<a id="file-readme-md"></a>

# Mapsui OGC Desktop Lab

A documented .NET 10 WPF GIS application for an air-gapped Windows workstation with internal Artifactory and existing OGC services. Start with invented local features, then connect WMS, WMTS, and WFS. No public basemap, database, or server installation is required.

**Start reading:** [Complete build guide](#contents). For shorter reading sessions use [lesson 01](#file-docs-chapters-01-foundations-md). Local coding models should start with [LLM context](#file-llm-context-md).

## AIS / ADS-B Traffic Lab

A separate [TrafficDemo.Wpf](#file-src-trafficdemo-wpf-trafficdemo-wpf-csproj) app simulates
configurable ship and aircraft populations, draws directional symbols and bounded
traces, supports selection/following and separate input/display filters, and measures
Mapsui rendering under sustained load. The validation target is 10,000 moving objects;
larger populations are allowed with a warning. It uses the existing local OSM
GeoServer at port 8602, with overlay-only mode for comparison.

```powershell
dotnet run --project src/TrafficDemo.Wpf -c Release
```

Configure the internal NuGet feed and restore as described below first. For published
launch, native smoke checks and reproducible benchmarks, start with the
[traffic viewer guide](#file-docs-chapters-12-traffic-viewer-md),
[pipeline and memory design](#file-docs-chapters-13-traffic-data-md), and
[performance guide](#file-docs-chapters-14-traffic-performance-md).

## Hello World: just a map

For the smallest example, open [HelloMap.Wpf](#file-src-hellomap-wpf-hellomap-wpf-csproj). It is a separate WPF app with one map control, one WMS basemap, and one in-memory polyline layer. Four white circles mark the line's vertices. All map setup is in [MainWindow.xaml.cs](#file-src-hellomap-wpf-mainwindow-xaml-cs); there is no view model, DI, or configuration framework. See the [Hello World README](#file-src-hellomap-wpf-readme-md) for setup and an explanation of its NuGet dependencies.

1. In `src/HelloMap.Wpf/MainWindow.xaml.cs`, replace `WmsUrl` and `WmsLayer` with your internal WMS endpoint and advertised layer name. The checked-in URL is a placeholder. This minimal example uses anonymous WMS 1.3.0 with `EPSG:3857` and `image/png`.
2. Edit the four `(longitude, latitude)` pairs if your basemap covers another area; the example line is near Tel Aviv. The code projects them to Web Mercator and zooms to the line.
3. Configure `NuGet.local.Config` as described below, then run on Windows:

```powershell
dotnet restore src/HelloMap.Wpf --configfile NuGet.local.Config --locked-mode
dotnet run --project src/HelloMap.Wpf --no-restore
```

Drag to pan and use the mouse wheel to zoom. The polyline appears immediately; the basemap loads asynchronously. If the WMS capabilities request fails, a message explains the failure and the polyline remains usable. A configured, reachable service covering the vertices is required to see the basemap. Use the full app below for authentication and other OGC features.

## Run on Windows

Use Windows PowerShell in a Windows-local copy of the project. Install your organization's .NET 10 SDK through its approved software channel first.

```powershell
Copy-Item NuGet.Config NuGet.local.Config
notepad NuGet.local.Config
# Replace only the example Artifactory URL with your internal NuGet v3 feed.
dotnet restore OgcDemo.slnx --configfile NuGet.local.Config --locked-mode
dotnet build OgcDemo.slnx --no-restore
dotnet run --project src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-build --no-restore
```

The two local layers load immediately. Drag to pan, wheel to zoom, and click a shape to inspect its attributes. The orange overlay marks the selected geometry. Enable your endpoints in a private copy of the configuration as described in the [configuration reference](#file-docs-configuration-md).

## What is implemented

- WPF/MVVM, DI, JSON configuration, daily file logging, layer tree and layer controls.
- Graphics and GeoJSON, points/lines/polygons, selection and independent highlighting.
- WMS 1.1.1/1.3.0, WMTS 1.0.0, WFS 1.1.0/2.0.0 with a bounded GML 3.1/3.2 subset.
- Capabilities checks, anonymous/Basic authentication, request limits, cancellation, debounced WFS fetching, and stale-response protection.
- EPSG:4326 local data transformed to EPSG:3857 display; service layers must advertise EPSG:3857.

[Advanced extensions](#file-docs-extensions-advanced-md) describe SQLite, GeoPackage, GDAL, persistent caching, search, and editing. These are design lessons, not completed core features. See [validation](#file-docs-validation-md) for precisely what was tested.

## Maintain the guide

```powershell
dotnet run --project tools/ManualBuilder --no-restore
dotnet run --project tools/ManualBuilder --no-restore -- --check
```

Edit authored prose under `docs/templates`, then regenerate. Lesson listings and the combined manual are generated from the actual source. The combined guide includes all source and configuration files needed to reconstruct the reference project; it is not dependent on opening external documentation.

Local-LLM documentation is project context only. This application does not contact or embed a model.


<a id="file-llm-context-md"></a>

# Local LLM entry point

Goal: help a human build and understand a .NET 10 WPF GIS client. The server is a generic existing OGC provider. Dependencies come from internal Artifactory. Do not add public basemaps or runtime internet dependencies.

Read `docs/configuration.md`, `docs/api-reference.md`, then the relevant numbered lesson. If only one file can be ingested, use `docs/BUILD-GUIDE.md`; its source sections use exact repository-relative paths. Use individual chapters for a smaller context window. Never assume an entire manual fits a model's context.

## Project map

| Area | Source | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| Composition | src/OgcDemo.Wpf/App.xaml.cs | Host, DI, configuration, file log, window lifecycle |
| UI | src/OgcDemo.Wpf/MainWindow.xaml | Map, layer tree, controls, details |
| View adapter | src/OgcDemo.Wpf/MainWindow.xaml.cs | Map tapped and tree selection events only |
| State | src/OgcDemo.Wpf/MainViewModel.cs | Commands, layer state, async refresh, selection |
| Local geometry | src/OgcDemo.Core/FeatureLayers.cs | GeoJSON, graphics, highlight cloning |
| Projection | src/OgcDemo.Core/Coordinates.cs | 4326 to/from 3857, bbox axis order |
| OGC raster | src/OgcDemo.Core/OgcLayers.cs | Mapsui WMS and BruTile WMTS adapters |
| OGC vector | src/OgcDemo.Core/WfsClient.cs | Capabilities, schema request, bounded GetFeature |
| GML | src/OgcDemo.Core/GmlFeatures.cs | Explicit 2D geometry subset |
| Transport | src/OgcDemo.Core/OgcTransport.cs | Credentials, same-origin requests, limits, safe XML |
| Concurrency | src/OgcDemo.Core/LatestRequest.cs | Generation checks and cancellation |
| Tests | tests/OgcDemo.Tests | Synthetic loopback HTTP fixtures and behavior tests |
| Manual generation | tools/ManualBuilder | Source-derived lessons and complete manual |

## Invariants

- WPF runs on Windows. Core tests can run on Linux; that does not prove WPF behavior.
- Mapsui package versions are 5.1.0; BruTile is 6.0.0. Do not mix v4 samples with these APIs.
- The Windows target includes OS version 10.0.19041.0 for compatible SkiaSharp assets.
- All map coordinates are EPSG:3857 metres. GeoJSON is RFC 7946 longitude/latitude. Assigning an SRID does not transform coordinates.
- WMS/WMTS pixels are not vector features. A WMS identify result is text, not automatically a selectable geometry.
- The selection layer stays above all user layers and is excluded from picking.
- UI collection mutations occur on the WPF dispatcher. Network awaits must not block it.
- Canceled/stale WFS responses must never replace the newest viewport's data.
- Only HTTPS is accepted in application configuration. HTTP loopback permission exists only in tests.
- Credentials are process environment variables; private config is ignored and excluded from publish output. Do not log URLs, headers, server bodies, or feature attributes.
- No direct PostGIS access, GeoServer REST calls, server provisioning, persistent caches, or WFS transactions in the core.
- Never claim work-service compatibility from local fixtures alone.

## Commands (Windows PowerShell, repository root)

```powershell
dotnet restore OgcDemo.slnx --configfile NuGet.local.Config --locked-mode
dotnet build OgcDemo.slnx --no-restore
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore
dotnet run --project src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-build --no-restore
dotnet run --project tools/ManualBuilder --no-restore -- --check
```

After code changes regenerate the guide, build, and run relevant tests. Describe implemented behavior separately from extension proposals. Use existing configuration models rather than inventing unrelated settings. If a service fails, inspect its advertised capabilities and the supported subset before changing coordinate math.

## Traffic Lab extension

`TrafficDemo.Core` owns decoded synthetic feeds, bounded latest-report ingestion,
tracking, ring histories, immutable snapshots and spatial indexes. `TrafficDemo.Wpf`
owns UI publication, selection/following, OGC basemap connections and completed-render
instrumentation. Read lessons 12–14 before changing the pipeline. Preserve distinct
input/display filter semantics, one pending UI snapshot, worker ownership and immutable
published geometry. The 10k population is a validation target, never a hard cap.
Use native Windows benchmark evidence for performance claims; core tests and Linux
cross-compilation do not demonstrate WPF rendering. Benchmark JSON distinguishes
functional completion, performance targets and evaluated versus unevaluated soaks.
The local OSM server is reused; do not reprovision its data or volumes.


<a id="file-docs-configuration-md"></a>

# Configuration contract

## Package feed

`NuGet.Config` clears inherited feeds and declares one placeholder Artifactory NuGet v3 endpoint. Copy it to ignored `NuGet.local.Config`, replace that endpoint, and pass `--configfile NuGet.local.Config` on restore. Keep `<clear />`. There is no public fallback. Package hashes and resolved versions are in committed `packages.lock.json` files.

Use your organization's credential provider or `NuGetPackageSourceCredentials_Artifactory` environment variable if the feed requires authentication. Do not put secrets into the template. NuGet's credential variable uses `Username=...;Password=...` and, when required by the feed, `ValidAuthenticationTypes=Basic`. Obtain credentials through your organization's normal procedure. Internal OGC credentials and Artifactory credentials are separate.

## Application settings

Copy the **whole** `src/OgcDemo.Wpf/appsettings.json` to `src/OgcDemo.Wpf/appsettings.local.json` before editing. JSON configuration arrays merge by numeric index; a short replacement Services array does not delete remaining base entries. Keep unused entries disabled. Restart after edits; configuration is read at startup.

| Setting | Meaning / default |
|---|---|
| Demo.InitialExtent | `[west,south,east,north]` in degrees; no dateline crossing |
| Services[].Name | Human-readable tree/selector label |
| Kind | Exactly `WMS`, `WMTS`, or `WFS` |
| Enabled | Exposes the profile in the selector; does not auto-connect |
| Url | Absolute HTTPS OGC endpoint, without username/password |
| Version | WMS 1.1.1/1.3.0; WMTS 1.0.0; WFS 1.1.0/2.0.0 |
| Layer | Advertised layer identifier or qualified WFS type name, case-sensitive |
| Style | WMS style (empty means default); exact advertised WMTS style |
| Crs | `EPSG:3857`; other service display CRSs are outside the core |
| MatrixSet | Exact WMTS matrix-set identifier, not a guessed zoom-level label |
| Format | WMS/WMTS advertised image type, normally `image/png`; WFS discovers GML output |
| Authentication | `Anonymous` or `Basic` |
| CredentialPrefix | Environment variable prefix, default `OGC_DEMO` |
| TimeoutSeconds | Whole-request deadline, 1–120; default 20 |
| MaxFeatures | WFS per-request cap, 1–10,000; default 1,000 |
| MaxResponseBytes | Per-response bound, 1 KiB–32 MiB; default 8 MiB |

Set `OGC_DEMO_USERNAME` and `OGC_DEMO_PASSWORD` only when using Basic with that prefix. The app reads them from its process environment. Do not enter passwords in commands saved to shell history; use an interactive credential prompt and assign its values to environment variables in the launching session. Clear those variables after the application exits.

Capabilities may advertise request endpoints different from the capabilities URL. The demo accepts a different path on the same scheme, host, and port; it rejects a different origin and HTTP redirects. Ask for a same-origin gateway if your deployment advertises multiple origins. It never forwards Basic credentials to an unapproved host.

## Data and scope limits

GeoJSON: FeatureCollection, RFC 7946 longitude/latitude, no legacy `crs` member, valid nonempty geometry, at most 8 MiB and 10,000 features. Latitude must be within approximately ±85.0511 degrees for Web Mercator.

WFS: HTTP GET, GML 3.1/3.2, 2D Point/LineString/Polygon (including holes), MultiPoint, MultiLineString/MultiCurve containing lines, and MultiPolygon/MultiSurface containing polygons. Curved segments, surface patches, complex nested attributes, remote XLinks, GML 2 `coordinates`, and 3D are unsupported. Only scalar feature properties are displayed. A layer at the feature cap is explicitly marked incomplete; the core does not silently paginate or claim complete coverage.

Logs: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OgcDemo\logs\ogc-demo-YYYYMMDD.log`, UTC timestamps. Daily filenames are rotation, not retention; see the deployment lesson. The logger omits exception payloads; callers must still use authored messages, never sensitive state.

## Traffic viewer configuration

The independent traffic app uses its own `Traffic` section and private
`appsettings.local.json` overlay. See [lesson 12](#file-docs-chapters-12-traffic-viewer-md) for all
simulation, history, filtering and basemap defaults. Only its OGC connection explicitly
permits loopback HTTP; the existing full OGC application's HTTPS validation is unchanged.


<a id="file-docs-api-reference-md"></a>

# Pinned APIs and references

Verified against Mapsui tag `5.1.0` and the restored packages. Documentation pages may advance beyond this release; the package lock and matching source tag take precedence.

| Task | API used |
|---|---|
| WPF control | `Mapsui.UI.Wpf.MapControl`, XAML assembly `Mapsui.UI.Wpf` |
| Map event | `Map.Tapped`, `MapEventArgs.GetMapInfo(layers)` |
| Layer picking | Explicit vector-layer list; do not copy v4 `IsMapInfoLayer` examples |
| Vector storage | `MemoryLayer.Features`, `DataHasChanged()` |
| NTS geometry | `Mapsui.Nts.GeometryFeature` |
| WMS | `Mapsui.Providers.Wms.WmsProvider(XmlDocument, getBytesAsync)` in Mapsui core |
| WMS image layer | `Mapsui.Layers.ImageLayer` |
| WMTS discovery | `BruTile.Wmts.WmtsCapabilitiesParser.Parse(Stream)` |
| WMTS selection | `WmtsTileSchema.Layer`, `Style`, `TileMatrixSet`, `Format`, `Srs` |
| Authenticated tiles | Override `HttpTileSource.GetTileAsync(HttpClient, TileInfo, CancellationToken?)` |
| Vector projection | NTS `ICoordinateSequenceFilter` and Mapsui `SphericalMercator` |

The WFS adapter owns HTTP requests rather than using Mapsui's built-in `WFSProvider`. Inspection of the pinned implementation found a fixed HTTP timeout and no caller cancellation/response-size interface. The small adapter gives this tutorial consistent transport limits and predictable generic-service behavior; it intentionally supports a documented GML subset, not all WFS encodings.

Direct package choices: Mapsui/Mapsui.Nts/Mapsui.Tiling/Mapsui.Wpf 5.1.0; BruTile 6.0.0; NetTopologySuite 2.6.0; GeoJSON4STJ 4.0.0; Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting and Logging.Abstractions 10.0.0; CommunityToolkit.Mvvm 8.4.0. Exact transitive versions and content hashes are recorded per project. The project files are the authoritative package list.

Primary references:

- [Mapsui 5.1.0 source](https://github.com/Mapsui/Mapsui/tree/5.1.0)
- [Mapsui WPF package](https://www.nuget.org/packages/Mapsui.Wpf/5.1.0)
- [BruTile 6.0.0 source](https://github.com/BruTile/BruTile/tree/6.0.0)
- [WPF overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/desktop/wpf/overview/)
- [WMS standard](https://www.ogc.org/standards/wms/)
- [WMTS standard](https://www.ogc.org/standards/wmts/)
- [WFS standard](https://www.ogc.org/standards/wfs/)
- [GeoJSON RFC 7946](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7946)

External references are supplementary. Core instructions, code, fixtures, configuration, and expected results are included locally for air-gapped use.

## Traffic Lab contracts

`ITrackFeed` supplies cancellable decoded `TrackReport` values. `TrafficEngine` owns
background simulation/tracking and emits immutable `TrafficSnapshot` values.
`SnapshotBuilder` creates read-only `RenderSnapshot` geometry/indexes.
`IndexedTrafficLayer` provides viewport queries to the standard Mapsui renderer.
`MeasuredRenderer` forwards renderer operations and measures completed drawing.
`TrackFilter` is shared by separate admission and display paths. See
[lesson 13](#file-docs-chapters-13-traffic-data-md) for ownership and ordering rules and
[lesson 14](#file-docs-chapters-14-traffic-performance-md) for JSON/CSV evidence semantics.

# Requirements coverage

The project follows the supplied email's application goals, with the agreed client-only OGC boundary and advanced topics as extension lessons.

| Requirement | Delivered coverage |
|---|---|
| Architecture and rationale | Lesson 01, project map, pinned API reference |
| Complete solution, csproj, App/MainWindow files | Complete generated source listings and runnable projects |
| MVVM, DI, logging, appsettings | Lesson 03 and configuration reference |
| Layer tree and dynamic management | Lesson 06; visibility, opacity, order, zoom, add/remove |
| WMS | Lesson 07; 1.1.1/1.3.0, map images, conditional GetFeatureInfo |
| WMTS | Lesson 08; 1.0.0, explicit style/matrix-set selection |
| WFS | Lesson 09; 1.1/2.0, bounded GML 3.1/3.2 subset |
| GeoJSON and graphics | Lesson 05; local points, lines, polygons and attributes |
| Selection and highlighting | Independent cloned selection overlay |
| Authentication | Anonymous/Basic over HTTPS; all request stages |
| Coordinate transformations | 4326/3857 plus tested protocol axis-order rules |
| GeoPackage recommendations | Advanced extension design and acceptance cases |
| SQLite and GDAL integration | Advanced extension designs, boundaries, and GDAL command example |
| Offline cache, search, editing | Advanced extension designs; not core implementations |
| Deployment and production guidance | Lesson 11 and enterprise integration extension |
| Troubleshooting and performance | Lesson 10, bounds, debounce and failure tests |
| GeoServer best practices | Reframed as generic OGC client/provider contract; server administration excluded |
| PostGIS | Possible server-side data store; no client database access |
| Package reference list | Project files, API reference, per-project locks |
| Human and local-LLM documentation | Eleven numbered lessons, compact LLM context, combined source-inclusive manual |

The local fixture server is test infrastructure only. It is not a substitute GIS server or an additional deployment dependency. Actual work-service URLs, authentication, data, and Artifactory access have not been supplied to this workspace.

# Validation record — 8 October 2026

## Verified results

| Check | Result | Evidence retained locally |
|---|---|---|
| Native Windows locked restore | Passed with the explicit development-validation feed | `.artifacts/windows/restore-windows.log` |
| Native Windows Release solution build | Passed, zero warnings and zero errors | `.artifacts/windows/build-windows.log` |
| Core and real loopback OGC fixture tests on Windows | **49 passed**, zero failed/skipped | `.artifacts/windows/windows-tests.trx` and `test-windows.log` |
| Framework-dependent win-x64 publish | Passed | `.artifacts/windows/publish-windows.log`; binaries in `.artifacts/publish` |
| Published Windows application integration smoke | **16 checks passed** | `.artifacts/windows/desktop-release.json` |
| Actual WPF render exported during smoke run | Inspected: points, line strokes, filled polygons, a polygon hole, selection highlight | `.artifacts/windows/desktop-release.png` |
| Generated documentation/source consistency | Passed on Linux and Windows; normalized LF output | `.artifacts/docs-check.log` and `.artifacts/windows/docs-windows.log` |
| Manual source listing comparison | All source blocks matched their current files after newline normalization | `.artifacts/manual-source-check.json` |

Windows validation used SDK 10.0.401, runtime 10.0.12, Windows x64 (OS version 10.0.26300). The SDK was extracted to a task-specific temporary directory after verifying its official SHA-512 hash; it was not installed system-wide. Linux cross-builds and earlier core tests also ran, but the table reports the final native Windows results. The Linux host lacked ICU, so early Linux tooling used invariant globalization; the native Windows tests did not use that workaround.

## What the tests exercise

Tests cover known-coordinate projection round trips, invalid coordinates, WMS/WFS axis order, JSON configuration binding, GeoJSON projection and polygon holes, geometry-specific styles, independent highlighting, layer order, stale-generation rejection, safe log behavior, and the supported GML geometry subset in both 3.1 and 3.2 encodings.

The fixture is a real loopback HTTP listener with synthetic XML, GML, and valid PNG responses. Protocol tests exercise both WMS versions, WMTS source selection and tile retrieval, both WFS versions, DescribeFeatureType, BBOX/feature limits, authentication on discovery and data requests, wrong CRS, malformed XML, service exceptions, HTTP errors, redirect rejection, header/body timeouts, oversized fixed-length and chunked responses, cancellation, and cross-origin rejection. Application configuration permits HTTPS only; loopback HTTP is explicitly enabled by the test harness.

The desktop smoke check creates a real WPF window. It verifies rendered point picking, line-interior picking, polygon-fill picking, selection/details/highlight behavior, clear-selection, visibility, opacity, layer ordering, addition/removal, and recovery from an unavailable service. It runs against the **published** application, not just the compiler output.

## Boundaries and remaining acceptance work

- **Manual physical-input walkthrough: pending.** The Computer Use runtime rejected this WSL workspace path (`sandboxCwd is not a local file URI`). Desktop coverage here is an in-process integration test plus inspection of its exported rendering. File-dialog interaction, physical mouse/keyboard navigation, display scaling, and accessibility still need the human checklist in lesson 11.
- **Internal Artifactory: not tested.** No internal endpoint was supplied. Development validation used an explicit ignored public NuGet configuration. The distributed configuration remains Artifactory-only with no fallback; lock files were preserved during the native Windows restore.
- **Actual internal OGC services and PKI: not tested.** No work URLs, credentials, or datasets were supplied. Fixtures establish the documented client behavior, not compatibility with every server, application schema, or enterprise certificate/proxy configuration.
- **Self-contained publishing: documented option, not the validated output.** The verified package is framework-dependent and needs the .NET 10 Desktop Runtime. Installers, signing, enterprise authentication beyond Basic, and production performance qualification remain extensions.

No work-server state was changed. Temporary build tools, binaries, logs, and reports are ignored by source control. Real credentials and private settings are not included in the project or generated manual.

## Traffic Lab validation — 2026-10-17

The traffic viewer is a separate application; the earlier OGC results above remain
historical evidence for that application. New traffic evidence is retained locally in
`.artifacts/traffic/`, with native Windows run reports, CSV samples and captured PNGs
under `.artifacts/traffic/windows/`. The test workstation was verified as an AMD Ryzen
9 9950X3D (16 cores / 32 logical processors), with approximately 61.3 GiB usable RAM,
Windows 10.0.26300, .NET 10.0.12 and Mapsui 5.1.0. The measured map viewport was
approximately 1106 ×607 WPF logical pixels at 120 DPI. These are workstation-specific
results, not a guarantee for other hardware or arbitrary history/report-rate settings.

### Build and functional checks

- Linux cross-build and native Windows Release build/publish succeeded. Core tests
  cover 18 traffic scenarios, including unclustered 12,000-object data. The existing
  OGC tests passed; an additional WMTS resource-selection regression raises that
  suite from 49 to 50 tests.
- Native smoke verified rendered symbols and picking, trace publication, selected
  details, following suspension, pause, separate input/display filters, reset/start,
  and recoverable service failure. The process exited cleanly after the shutdown fix.
  These are in-process checks, not physical mouse/keyboard automation.
- The running Eastern Europe OSM GeoServer was reused. Both Windows and WSL could
  fetch its capabilities; the corrected WMTS integration received actual map images.
  The helper's healthy-service path and script syntax were checked. The stopped-server
  start path was not exercised against the live service; it was unnecessary to stop it.
- Visual inspection of native captures confirmed directional cyan ships, amber
  aircraft, the OSM basemap and the population table. Overlap in the dense scenario
  is intentional; counts are not reduced by clustering.

The 30-second dense 10,000-object check with traces and GeoServer recorded all 10,000
symbols, zero coalesced/capacity-rejected reports, zero basemap failures, p95/p99
whole-run ingestion-to-render upper bounds of **1.131 / 1.212 seconds**, p95 render
upper bound **127 ms**, and p95 dispatcher upper bound **102 ms**. All applicable
short-run targets passed. This short run alone does **not** establish soak stability.

### Integration defects found during validation

The implementation was corrected using actual native/service evidence:

1. GeoServer advertises an empty WMTS style identifier; BruTile normalizes it to
   `default`, so the viewer selects that normalized value.
2. GeoServer also advertises TileJSON/FeatureInfo resources. BruTile 6 could select
   those templates as tile requests, producing incomplete basemaps. The shared adapter
   now passes only tile resources for the configured image format to the parser.
   A fixture regression verifies that auxiliary endpoints are never requested as tiles.
3. A synchronously completed disposal could reenter the WPF Closing event. Final
   close is now dispatched after the event returns; runners check process exit codes.

Earlier diagnostic runs with missing tiles or failed shutdown are not final acceptance
results. Their existence is not evidence of a passing GeoServer integration.

Native execution used a Windows-local source copy and a portable SDK. A copied
cross-built DLL was rejected by this workstation's Application Control during an
intermediate attempt; the native Windows build ran successfully. No security settings
were changed. Use the documented Windows build workflow when reproducing these results.

### Completed 30-minute soak

The native Release fixed-view run used 5,000 ships plus 5,000 aircraft, dense mode,
normal 5 s / 2 s reporting, five-minute / 150-point histories, traces enabled and the
live GeoServer WMTS basemap. It completed in 1,800.17 seconds and exited with code 0.
Evidence: `.artifacts/traffic/windows/soak-10k-1800s.{json,csv,png}`.

| Whole-run measurement | Result |
| --- | ---: |
| Generated and accepted reports | 6,224,391 each |
| Rejected / coalesced / capacity-rejected reports | 0 / 0 / 0 |
| Tracked and dense-view symbols observed | 10,000 / 10,000 |
| Retained history points at completion | 1,036,453 |
| Ingestion-to-render p95 / p99 upper bounds | 1,259 / 1,333 ms |
| Dispatcher p95 upper bound | 111 ms |
| Mapsui render p95 upper bound | 136 ms |
| Snapshot preparation p95 upper bound | 155 ms |
| UI publication p95 upper bound | 6 ms |
| Displayed-report age p95 / p99 upper bounds | 4.77 / 5.17 s |
| Basemap image responses / request failures | 32 / 0 |
| Replaced pending snapshots | 0 |
| Private memory at completion | 489.7 MiB |
| Private-memory trend after minute 10 | +0.539 MiB/min |

All recorded bounds and latency targets passed. The memory trend passed the explicit
1 MiB/min diagnostic threshold; post-minute-10 private bytes ranged approximately
470–521 MiB and fluctuated around a plateau. This does not prove the absence of every
possible leak. Displayed-report age includes the five-second ship cadence and is
separate from ingestion latency. Stored history was not disabled; simplification
reduces the displayed vertices independently of the retained-point count.

The application allocated about 164 GiB cumulatively over this run (roughly 93 MiB/s),
while retained process memory stayed bounded. This distinguishes allocation churn
from a retention leak and is a useful optimization target for future work. The run
used about 601 CPU seconds over 1,800 wall seconds. No other traffic benchmark was
run concurrently. Server cache state was retained; the application began with an
empty client cache. The PNG shows all symbols submitted in the dense viewport, with
intentional heavy overlap along the synthetic corridors.

### Population/type and basemap comparisons

All **18 native Release cases passed** functional checks, full-population/dense-view
checks, latency/dispatcher targets, bounded-state checks and basemap health. Each
case ran for 30 seconds in a fresh process, with dense mode and traces enabled.
The early interaction steps select/follow a track and return home. The full interaction
cycle is checked separately in a longer run. The committed runner defaults to
60 seconds; this recorded comparison explicitly used `-Seconds 30`.

Evidence: `.artifacts/traffic/windows/matrix/`, including `summary.json` and separate
JSON/CSV/PNG files for every case. Values below are conservative whole-run histogram
upper bounds in milliseconds. The dispatcher column is the worse p95 of the two
basemap modes for that row.

| Objects | Population | GeoServer ingestion p95 / p99 | Overlay ingestion p95 / p99 | Dispatcher p95 max |
| ---: | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 1,000 | ships | 1085 / 3739 | 1085 / 3513 | 14 |
| 1,000 | aircraft | 1116 / 1775 | 1071 / 1572 | 15 |
| 1,000 | mixed | 1077 / 1427 | 1077 / 1426 | 9 |
| 5,000 | ships | 1148 / 3832 | 1157 / 3920 | 53 |
| 5,000 | aircraft | 1183 / 1865 | 1185 / 1801 | 46 |
| 5,000 | mixed | 1116 / 1862 | 1138 / 2122 | 44 |
| 10,000 | ships | 1217 / 4013 | 1209 / 3946 | 104 |
| 10,000 | aircraft | 1231 / 1907 | 1234 / 1889 | 97 |
| 10,000 | mixed | 1158 / 1975 | 1191 / 1994 | 130 |

The all-ships interaction p99 is higher than the fixed-view soak: returning home
introduces reports that were offscreen during the focused view, with a five-second
source cadence. These samples remain included. No objects or traces were silently
removed to pass a target. Short comparison runs do not replace the 30-minute soak.

### Additional views, traces and interaction checks

These final native Release runs all passed functional and applicable performance
checks. Values are whole-run conservative upper bounds in milliseconds. Each is a
fresh process. The six 20-second 10k comparisons complete the dense/dispersed,
traces-on/off and GeoServer/overlay combinations for the mixed 10,000-object case;
they are not a claim that the optional full 72-case Cartesian matrix was executed.

The 65-second interaction run includes selection/following, zoom-to-object, home,
display filtering, restoring the full display, and panning. The 12,000-object run
tracked and drew all 12,000 objects; the visible warning did not impose a 10,000 cap.
The native functional smoke (`smoke-final.json`) passed **14 checks** and exited 0.
Its deliberate pause/reset and failed-service injection make its latency and basemap
health acceptance fields fail; it is not a steady-load performance acceptance run.
The baseline comparisons and soak above have zero basemap failures.

Final Windows build: **zero warnings/errors; 68 tests passed** (18 traffic, 50 OGC).
The final WPF binary includes an additional startup-only guard that refuses to overwrite
an existing benchmark report even through the error path. The soak and baseline matrix
predate that guard; tracking, rendering, measurement and OGC logic are unchanged.
The core DLL hashes are identical; source/binary hashes and build/test evidence are
retained in `run-manifest.json` and the native logs/TRX files.

The final live **WMS** check (1,000 objects, regional view, 20 seconds) also passed
with image responses and zero basemap failures (`wms-final.json`). The original OGC
application's newly published native smoke passed **all 16 checks** (`ogc-final.json`).
A separate startup regression preserved an existing report byte-for-byte and returned
the expected refusal exit code 1 (`report-preservation-check.json`).

Generated documentation and full source listings are checked against the current
files. Private configuration, binaries and machine-specific reports remain ignored;
the unrelated pre-existing `docs/nuget-packages.md` was not changed. Physical
mouse/keyboard/file-dialog testing remains unverified because the available Computer

<a id="file-docs-extensions-advanced-md"></a>

# Advanced extension lessons

These are extension designs and exercises. They are deliberately **not implemented** by the core demo. Finish the working OGC/local-feature lessons first. Each extension has a concrete integration point and an acceptance test so a human or local model can continue without confusing proposed behavior with existing functionality.

## SQLite — persist user preferences

**Goal:** restore user-created layer profiles and visibility/order after restarting. Start with ordinary application tables, not spatial storage.

1. Add a pinned SQLite provider available in Artifactory to a separate persistence project. Keep native runtime assets explicit for win-x64.
2. Introduce an `ILayerProfileStore` with asynchronous load/save operations. Store profile identifiers, display settings, and a schema version; exclude passwords and access tokens.
3. Use parameterized SQL, transactions for a whole workspace save, and a migration table. Place the database under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OgcDemo`, never beside a read-only installed EXE.
4. Load persisted settings at startup before adding layers. Save only explicit user settings, not transient highlights or all fetched WFS data.
5. Test first launch, restart, interrupted writes, schema migration, and a read-only directory. A corrupt store must not prevent the bundled local demo from starting.

## GeoPackage — read an offline vector dataset

**Goal:** load a selected GeoPackage feature table by viewport. A `.gpkg` is a SQLite container with spatial metadata and geometry encoding; it is not equivalent to an arbitrary table of WKB.

1. Select a vetted provider that understands GeoPackage metadata, geometry headers, and spatial indexes. Verify its support on the chosen .NET/Windows versions before adding it.
2. Read `gpkg_contents`, geometry-column metadata, source CRS, and bounds. Reject a table with an unknown CRS rather than assuming 4326.
3. Add a data-source adapter returning `GeometryFeature` objects in the map CRS. Use a spatial index and a bounded query for the current extent.
4. Keep tiles and vector tables as separate capabilities; a provider supporting vector tables does not necessarily support GeoPackage raster tiles.
5. Test multiple geometry types, polygon holes, empty/null geometry, missing indexes, multiple tables, and a non-4326 dataset. Compare transformed control points with a trusted GIS tool.

For small teaching inputs, converting a selected table to RFC 7946 GeoJSON through an approved conversion tool is a simpler first exercise. Do not claim native GeoPackage support merely because an export can be loaded.

## GDAL — controlled preprocessing

**Goal:** prepare large or unfamiliar source formats outside the UI rendering loop.

1. Use the organization's approved GDAL distribution. Keep its executables, native libraries, PROJ data, and driver versions together. Verify `ogrinfo --version` and the relevant driver's availability.
2. Inspect a source layer and its CRS before converting. Example commands below are **GDAL shell commands**, not application features:

```powershell
ogrinfo -so -al source.gpkg
ogr2ogr -f GeoJSON -t_srs EPSG:4326 -lco RFC7946=YES training-export.geojson source.gpkg selected_table
```

Use a new destination filename. Inspect feature count, bounds, and representative geometries before importing into the demo.

3. If later integrating conversion into the app, execute a configured tool path with `ProcessStartInfo.ArgumentList`; do not concatenate untrusted filenames into a shell command.
4. Run conversion off the dispatcher, capture bounded diagnostic output, allow cancellation, and preserve the original source.
5. Test filenames with spaces, invalid CRS, missing projection grids, unavailable drivers, cancellation, and nonzero exit codes. Log operation results without recording sensitive paths or features.

## Persistent offline cache — separate network isolation from disconnection

**Goal:** allow a deliberately prepared map area to remain available when internal services are unreachable.

1. Define user-selected area, scales, layers, storage limit, expiry, and whether the service permits caching. Air-gapped access to an internal server does not require prefetching by itself.
2. Start with a WMTS tile cache; include service/profile identity, layer, style, format, matrix-set identifier, matrix level, row/column, and authorization scope in the cache key.
3. Store a manifest with extent, CRS, creation time, provenance, and completeness. Never show a partial package as a complete offline map.
4. Separate authenticated users' caches. Credentials are never cache keys or stored payload metadata. Define invalidation and deletion behavior explicitly.
5. Add explicit online/offline mode and a visible missing-data indication. Bound disk use and concurrent downloads; do not launch an unbounded tile crawl.
6. Test disconnected restart, missing tiles, expired entries, changed styles, user changes, interrupted prefetch, and full disk. Check both rendered coverage and manifest counts.

WFS snapshots need their own data-version and edit-conflict policies. WMS viewport-image caching is not interchangeable with WMTS tile caching.

## Search — visible-data search first

**Goal:** find features by their displayed attributes and navigate to them.

1. Add an `IFeatureSearch` interface and a local implementation over loaded vector features. Return a stable feature reference, layer identity, label, and extent.
2. Clearly label the first implementation "Search loaded features"; a bounded WFS viewport is not a complete catalog.
3. Keep filtering and result limits explicit. For server search, discover supported filters and use standard OGC Filter Encoding rather than assuming GeoServer CQL support.
4. Debounce input, cancel previous requests, and reuse the generation-check pattern. Do not block typing on network work.
5. Test duplicate labels, missing attributes, Unicode text, empty results, stale requests, and selecting a result whose layer was removed.

## Editing — local geometry before remote transactions

**Goal:** edit a local copy with undo/redo, validation, and explicit save.

1. Keep selection, editing, and persisted source geometry separate. Begin with adding/moving points; extend to lines/polygons only after undo is reliable.
2. Define commands containing old/new geometry, stable feature identity, and layer identity. Reproject at input/output boundaries.
3. Validate topology before committing edits. Invalid polygon rings must not silently reach the data store.
4. Save a new local GeoJSON file with confirmed destination semantics before attempting server writes. The core demo currently never writes back to an input dataset.
5. Add WFS-T only when the service advertises it and a separate explicit authorization/conflict design exists. Reading WFS does not imply write access.
6. Test undo/redo, canceled edits, invalid geometry, lost selection, save failure, concurrent modification, and CRS round trips.

## Enterprise server integration and deployment

The generic client contract is capabilities plus data requests. Ask the provider's administrator for advertised CRS/format support, layer permissions, bounded WFS access, correct externally advertised operation URLs, and reliable HTTPS. GeoServer can satisfy this contract, but the client does not need its REST API, configuration files, or a PostGIS account.

Production extensions include Windows Integrated Authentication or OAuth, enterprise proxy handling, credential-vault integration, signed packaging, automated update policy, log retention, and accessibility/scaling testing. Implement each against the actual organization's contract and validate it separately. Do not add server provisioning to this client tutorial.

### `LLM_CONTEXT.md`

Source SHA-256: `f106bd31edee37a2b68abc0e87a29e0814cc73a2fd1d38556675e8fbba6c9713`

``````text
# Local LLM entry point

Goal: help a human build and understand a .NET 10 WPF GIS client. The server is a generic existing OGC provider. Dependencies come from internal Artifactory. Do not add public basemaps or runtime internet dependencies.

Read `docs/configuration.md`, `docs/api-reference.md`, then the relevant numbered lesson. If only one file can be ingested, use `docs/BUILD-GUIDE.md`; its source sections use exact repository-relative paths. Use individual chapters for a smaller context window. Never assume an entire manual fits a model's context.

## Project map

| Area | Source | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| Composition | src/OgcDemo.Wpf/App.xaml.cs | Host, DI, configuration, file log, window lifecycle |
| UI | src/OgcDemo.Wpf/MainWindow.xaml | Map, layer tree, controls, details |
| View adapter | src/OgcDemo.Wpf/MainWindow.xaml.cs | Map tapped and tree selection events only |
| State | src/OgcDemo.Wpf/MainViewModel.cs | Commands, layer state, async refresh, selection |
| Local geometry | src/OgcDemo.Core/FeatureLayers.cs | GeoJSON, graphics, highlight cloning |
| Projection | src/OgcDemo.Core/Coordinates.cs | 4326 to/from 3857, bbox axis order |
| OGC raster | src/OgcDemo.Core/OgcLayers.cs | Mapsui WMS and BruTile WMTS adapters |
| OGC vector | src/OgcDemo.Core/WfsClient.cs | Capabilities, schema request, bounded GetFeature |
| GML | src/OgcDemo.Core/GmlFeatures.cs | Explicit 2D geometry subset |
| Transport | src/OgcDemo.Core/OgcTransport.cs | Credentials, same-origin requests, limits, safe XML |
| Concurrency | src/OgcDemo.Core/LatestRequest.cs | Generation checks and cancellation |
| Tests | tests/OgcDemo.Tests | Synthetic loopback HTTP fixtures and behavior tests |
| Manual generation | tools/ManualBuilder | Source-derived lessons and complete manual |

## Invariants

- WPF runs on Windows. Core tests can run on Linux; that does not prove WPF behavior.
- Mapsui package versions are 5.1.0; BruTile is 6.0.0. Do not mix v4 samples with these APIs.
- The Windows target includes OS version 10.0.19041.0 for compatible SkiaSharp assets.
- All map coordinates are EPSG:3857 metres. GeoJSON is RFC 7946 longitude/latitude. Assigning an SRID does not transform coordinates.
- WMS/WMTS pixels are not vector features. A WMS identify result is text, not automatically a selectable geometry.
- The selection layer stays above all user layers and is excluded from picking.
- UI collection mutations occur on the WPF dispatcher. Network awaits must not block it.
- Canceled/stale WFS responses must never replace the newest viewport's data.
- Only HTTPS is accepted in application configuration. HTTP loopback permission exists only in tests.
- Credentials are process environment variables; private config is ignored and excluded from publish output. Do not log URLs, headers, server bodies, or feature attributes.
- No direct PostGIS access, GeoServer REST calls, server provisioning, persistent caches, or WFS transactions in the core.
- Never claim work-service compatibility from local fixtures alone.

## Commands (Windows PowerShell, repository root)

```powershell
dotnet restore OgcDemo.slnx --configfile NuGet.local.Config --locked-mode
dotnet build OgcDemo.slnx --no-restore
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore
dotnet run --project src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-build --no-restore
dotnet run --project tools/ManualBuilder --no-restore -- --check
```

After code changes regenerate the guide, build, and run relevant tests. Describe implemented behavior separately from extension proposals. Use existing configuration models rather than inventing unrelated settings. If a service fails, inspect its advertised capabilities and the supported subset before changing coordinate math.

## Traffic Lab extension

`TrafficDemo.Core` owns decoded synthetic feeds, bounded latest-report ingestion,
tracking, ring histories, immutable snapshots and spatial indexes. `TrafficDemo.Wpf`
owns UI publication, selection/following, OGC basemap connections and completed-render
instrumentation. Read lessons 12–14 before changing the pipeline. Preserve distinct
input/display filter semantics, one pending UI snapshot, worker ownership and immutable
published geometry. The 10k population is a validation target, never a hard cap.
Use native Windows benchmark evidence for performance claims; core tests and Linux
cross-compilation do not demonstrate WPF rendering. Benchmark JSON distinguishes
functional completion, performance targets and evaluated versus unevaluated soaks.
The local OSM server is reused; do not reprovision its data or volumes.
``````

<a id="file-readme-md-raw"></a>

### `README.md`

Source SHA-256: `a773d2b8a944f63bb56f4abe57af3c247e6a955b3d3b1ffa4f7bddacfc4d5050`

``````text
# Mapsui OGC Desktop Lab

A documented .NET 10 WPF GIS application for an air-gapped Windows workstation with internal Artifactory and existing OGC services. Start with invented local features, then connect WMS, WMTS, and WFS. No public basemap, database, or server installation is required.

**Start reading:** [Complete build guide](docs/BUILD-GUIDE.md). For shorter reading sessions use [lesson 01](docs/chapters/01-foundations.md). Local coding models should start with [LLM context](LLM_CONTEXT.md).

## AIS / ADS-B Traffic Lab

A separate [TrafficDemo.Wpf](src/TrafficDemo.Wpf/TrafficDemo.Wpf.csproj) app simulates
configurable ship and aircraft populations, draws directional symbols and bounded
traces, supports selection/following and separate input/display filters, and measures
Mapsui rendering under sustained load. The validation target is 10,000 moving objects;
larger populations are allowed with a warning. It uses the existing local OSM
GeoServer at port 8602, with overlay-only mode for comparison.

```powershell
dotnet run --project src/TrafficDemo.Wpf -c Release
```

Configure the internal NuGet feed and restore as described below first. For published
launch, native smoke checks and reproducible benchmarks, start with the
[traffic viewer guide](docs/chapters/12-traffic-viewer.md),
[pipeline and memory design](docs/chapters/13-traffic-data.md), and
[performance guide](docs/chapters/14-traffic-performance.md).

## Hello World: just a map

For the smallest example, open [HelloMap.Wpf](src/HelloMap.Wpf/HelloMap.Wpf.csproj). It is a separate WPF app with one map control, one WMS basemap, and one in-memory polyline layer. Four white circles mark the line's vertices. All map setup is in [MainWindow.xaml.cs](src/HelloMap.Wpf/MainWindow.xaml.cs); there is no view model, DI, or configuration framework. See the [Hello World README](src/HelloMap.Wpf/README.md) for setup and an explanation of its NuGet dependencies.

1. In `src/HelloMap.Wpf/MainWindow.xaml.cs`, replace `WmsUrl` and `WmsLayer` with your internal WMS endpoint and advertised layer name. The checked-in URL is a placeholder. This minimal example uses anonymous WMS 1.3.0 with `EPSG:3857` and `image/png`.
2. Edit the four `(longitude, latitude)` pairs if your basemap covers another area; the example line is near Tel Aviv. The code projects them to Web Mercator and zooms to the line.
3. Configure `NuGet.local.Config` as described below, then run on Windows:

```powershell
dotnet restore src/HelloMap.Wpf --configfile NuGet.local.Config --locked-mode
dotnet run --project src/HelloMap.Wpf --no-restore
```

Drag to pan and use the mouse wheel to zoom. The polyline appears immediately; the basemap loads asynchronously. If the WMS capabilities request fails, a message explains the failure and the polyline remains usable. A configured, reachable service covering the vertices is required to see the basemap. Use the full app below for authentication and other OGC features.

## Run on Windows

Use Windows PowerShell in a Windows-local copy of the project. Install your organization's .NET 10 SDK through its approved software channel first.

```powershell
Copy-Item NuGet.Config NuGet.local.Config
notepad NuGet.local.Config
# Replace only the example Artifactory URL with your internal NuGet v3 feed.
dotnet restore OgcDemo.slnx --configfile NuGet.local.Config --locked-mode
dotnet build OgcDemo.slnx --no-restore
dotnet run --project src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-build --no-restore
```

The two local layers load immediately. Drag to pan, wheel to zoom, and click a shape to inspect its attributes. The orange overlay marks the selected geometry. Enable your endpoints in a private copy of the configuration as described in the [configuration reference](docs/configuration.md).

## What is implemented

- WPF/MVVM, DI, JSON configuration, daily file logging, layer tree and layer controls.
- Graphics and GeoJSON, points/lines/polygons, selection and independent highlighting.
- WMS 1.1.1/1.3.0, WMTS 1.0.0, WFS 1.1.0/2.0.0 with a bounded GML 3.1/3.2 subset.
- Capabilities checks, anonymous/Basic authentication, request limits, cancellation, debounced WFS fetching, and stale-response protection.
- EPSG:4326 local data transformed to EPSG:3857 display; service layers must advertise EPSG:3857.

[Advanced extensions](docs/extensions/advanced.md) describe SQLite, GeoPackage, GDAL, persistent caching, search, and editing. These are design lessons, not completed core features. See [validation](docs/validation.md) for precisely what was tested.

## Maintain the guide

```powershell
dotnet run --project tools/ManualBuilder --no-restore
dotnet run --project tools/ManualBuilder --no-restore -- --check
```

Edit authored prose under `docs/templates`, then regenerate. Lesson listings and the combined manual are generated from the actual source. The combined guide includes all source and configuration files needed to reconstruct the reference project; it is not dependent on opening external documentation.

Local-LLM documentation is project context only. This application does not contact or embed a model.
``````
