### `docs/api-reference.md`

Source SHA-256: `30f171b1d3b82cfb30d3156996c675c974764f469c1f919e9c08426217260d79`

``````text
# Pinned APIs and references

Verified against Mapsui tag `5.1.0` and the restored packages. Documentation pages may advance beyond this release; the package lock and matching source tag take precedence.

| Task | API used |
|---|---|
| WPF control | `Mapsui.UI.Wpf.MapControl`, XAML assembly `Mapsui.UI.Wpf` |
| Map event | `Map.Tapped`, `MapEventArgs.GetMapInfo(layers)` |
| Layer picking | Explicit vector-layer list; do not copy v4 `IsMapInfoLayer` examples |
| Vector storage | `MemoryLayer.Features`, `DataHasChanged()` |
| NTS geometry | `Mapsui.Nts.GeometryFeature` |
| WMS | `Mapsui.Providers.Wms.WmsProvider(XmlDocument, getBytesAsync)` in Mapsui core |
| WMS image layer | `Mapsui.Layers.ImageLayer` |
| WMTS discovery | `BruTile.Wmts.WmtsCapabilitiesParser.Parse(Stream)` |
| WMTS selection | `WmtsTileSchema.Layer`, `Style`, `TileMatrixSet`, `Format`, `Srs` |
| Authenticated tiles | Override `HttpTileSource.GetTileAsync(HttpClient, TileInfo, CancellationToken?)` |
| Vector projection | NTS `ICoordinateSequenceFilter` and Mapsui `SphericalMercator` |

The WFS adapter owns HTTP requests rather than using Mapsui's built-in `WFSProvider`. Inspection of the pinned implementation found a fixed HTTP timeout and no caller cancellation/response-size interface. The small adapter gives this tutorial consistent transport limits and predictable generic-service behavior; it intentionally supports a documented GML subset, not all WFS encodings.

Direct package choices: Mapsui/Mapsui.Nts/Mapsui.Tiling/Mapsui.Wpf 5.1.0; BruTile 6.0.0; NetTopologySuite 2.6.0; GeoJSON4STJ 4.0.0; Microsoft.Extensions.Hosting and Logging.Abstractions 10.0.0; CommunityToolkit.Mvvm 8.4.0. Exact transitive versions and content hashes are recorded per project. The project files are the authoritative package list.

Primary references:

- [Mapsui 5.1.0 source](https://github.com/Mapsui/Mapsui/tree/5.1.0)
- [Mapsui WPF package](https://www.nuget.org/packages/Mapsui.Wpf/5.1.0)
- [BruTile 6.0.0 source](https://github.com/BruTile/BruTile/tree/6.0.0)
- [WPF overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/desktop/wpf/overview/)
- [WMS standard](https://www.ogc.org/standards/wms/)
- [WMTS standard](https://www.ogc.org/standards/wmts/)
- [WFS standard](https://www.ogc.org/standards/wfs/)
- [GeoJSON RFC 7946](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc7946)

External references are supplementary. Core instructions, code, fixtures, configuration, and expected results are included locally for air-gapped use.

## Traffic Lab contracts

`ITrackFeed` supplies cancellable decoded `TrackReport` values. `TrafficEngine` owns
background simulation/tracking and emits immutable `TrafficSnapshot` values.
`SnapshotBuilder` creates read-only `RenderSnapshot` geometry/indexes.
`IndexedTrafficLayer` provides viewport queries to the standard Mapsui renderer.
`MeasuredRenderer` forwards renderer operations and measures completed drawing.
`TrackFilter` is shared by separate admission and display paths. See
[lesson 13](chapters/13-traffic-data.md) for ownership and ordering rules and
[lesson 14](chapters/14-traffic-performance.md) for JSON/CSV evidence semantics.
``````

<a id="file-docs-configuration-md-raw"></a>

### `docs/configuration.md`

Source SHA-256: `e5b550d99a90f3f03aa6616e36df4d184dbff933c6b84e29940849e0c03ade2f`

``````text
# Configuration contract

## Package feed

`NuGet.Config` clears inherited feeds and declares one placeholder Artifactory NuGet v3 endpoint. Copy it to ignored `NuGet.local.Config`, replace that endpoint, and pass `--configfile NuGet.local.Config` on restore. Keep `<clear />`. There is no public fallback. Package hashes and resolved versions are in committed `packages.lock.json` files.

Use your organization's credential provider or `NuGetPackageSourceCredentials_Artifactory` environment variable if the feed requires authentication. Do not put secrets into the template. NuGet's credential variable uses `Username=...;Password=...` and, when required by the feed, `ValidAuthenticationTypes=Basic`. Obtain credentials through your organization's normal procedure. Internal OGC credentials and Artifactory credentials are separate.

## Application settings

Copy the **whole** `src/OgcDemo.Wpf/appsettings.json` to `src/OgcDemo.Wpf/appsettings.local.json` before editing. JSON configuration arrays merge by numeric index; a short replacement Services array does not delete remaining base entries. Keep unused entries disabled. Restart after edits; configuration is read at startup.

| Setting | Meaning / default |
|---|---|
| Demo.InitialExtent | `[west,south,east,north]` in degrees; no dateline crossing |
| Services[].Name | Human-readable tree/selector label |
| Kind | Exactly `WMS`, `WMTS`, or `WFS` |
| Enabled | Exposes the profile in the selector; does not auto-connect |
| Url | Absolute HTTPS OGC endpoint, without username/password |
| Version | WMS 1.1.1/1.3.0; WMTS 1.0.0; WFS 1.1.0/2.0.0 |
| Layer | Advertised layer identifier or qualified WFS type name, case-sensitive |
| Style | WMS style (empty means default); exact advertised WMTS style |
| Crs | `EPSG:3857`; other service display CRSs are outside the core |
| MatrixSet | Exact WMTS matrix-set identifier, not a guessed zoom-level label |
| Format | WMS/WMTS advertised image type, normally `image/png`; WFS discovers GML output |
| Authentication | `Anonymous` or `Basic` |
| CredentialPrefix | Environment variable prefix, default `OGC_DEMO` |
| TimeoutSeconds | Whole-request deadline, 1–120; default 20 |
| MaxFeatures | WFS per-request cap, 1–10,000; default 1,000 |
| MaxResponseBytes | Per-response bound, 1 KiB–32 MiB; default 8 MiB |

Set `OGC_DEMO_USERNAME` and `OGC_DEMO_PASSWORD` only when using Basic with that prefix. The app reads them from its process environment. Do not enter passwords in commands saved to shell history; use an interactive credential prompt and assign its values to environment variables in the launching session. Clear those variables after the application exits.

Capabilities may advertise request endpoints different from the capabilities URL. The demo accepts a different path on the same scheme, host, and port; it rejects a different origin and HTTP redirects. Ask for a same-origin gateway if your deployment advertises multiple origins. It never forwards Basic credentials to an unapproved host.

## Data and scope limits

GeoJSON: FeatureCollection, RFC 7946 longitude/latitude, no legacy `crs` member, valid nonempty geometry, at most 8 MiB and 10,000 features. Latitude must be within approximately ±85.0511 degrees for Web Mercator.

WFS: HTTP GET, GML 3.1/3.2, 2D Point/LineString/Polygon (including holes), MultiPoint, MultiLineString/MultiCurve containing lines, and MultiPolygon/MultiSurface containing polygons. Curved segments, surface patches, complex nested attributes, remote XLinks, GML 2 `coordinates`, and 3D are unsupported. Only scalar feature properties are displayed. A layer at the feature cap is explicitly marked incomplete; the core does not silently paginate or claim complete coverage.

Logs: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OgcDemo\logs\ogc-demo-YYYYMMDD.log`, UTC timestamps. Daily filenames are rotation, not retention; see the deployment lesson. The logger omits exception payloads; callers must still use authored messages, never sensitive state.

## Traffic viewer configuration

The independent traffic app uses its own `Traffic` section and private
`appsettings.local.json` overlay. See [lesson 12](chapters/12-traffic-viewer.md) for all
simulation, history, filtering and basemap defaults. Only its OGC connection explicitly
permits loopback HTTP; the existing full OGC application's HTTPS validation is unchanged.
``````

<a id="file-docs-extensions-advanced-md-raw"></a>

### `docs/extensions/advanced.md`

Source SHA-256: `ce5b0b093ee44f76fb019986caa21b6f20f1bf6b3483fc49cc2cb016a8fb13e5`

``````text
# Advanced extension lessons

These are extension designs and exercises. They are deliberately **not implemented** by the core demo. Finish the working OGC/local-feature lessons first. Each extension has a concrete integration point and an acceptance test so a human or local model can continue without confusing proposed behavior with existing functionality.

## SQLite — persist user preferences

**Goal:** restore user-created layer profiles and visibility/order after restarting. Start with ordinary application tables, not spatial storage.

1. Add a pinned SQLite provider available in Artifactory to a separate persistence project. Keep native runtime assets explicit for win-x64.
2. Introduce an `ILayerProfileStore` with asynchronous load/save operations. Store profile identifiers, display settings, and a schema version; exclude passwords and access tokens.
3. Use parameterized SQL, transactions for a whole workspace save, and a migration table. Place the database under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OgcDemo`, never beside a read-only installed EXE.
4. Load persisted settings at startup before adding layers. Save only explicit user settings, not transient highlights or all fetched WFS data.
5. Test first launch, restart, interrupted writes, schema migration, and a read-only directory. A corrupt store must not prevent the bundled local demo from starting.

## GeoPackage — read an offline vector dataset

**Goal:** load a selected GeoPackage feature table by viewport. A `.gpkg` is a SQLite container with spatial metadata and geometry encoding; it is not equivalent to an arbitrary table of WKB.

1. Select a vetted provider that understands GeoPackage metadata, geometry headers, and spatial indexes. Verify its support on the chosen .NET/Windows versions before adding it.
2. Read `gpkg_contents`, geometry-column metadata, source CRS, and bounds. Reject a table with an unknown CRS rather than assuming 4326.
3. Add a data-source adapter returning `GeometryFeature` objects in the map CRS. Use a spatial index and a bounded query for the current extent.
4. Keep tiles and vector tables as separate capabilities; a provider supporting vector tables does not necessarily support GeoPackage raster tiles.
5. Test multiple geometry types, polygon holes, empty/null geometry, missing indexes, multiple tables, and a non-4326 dataset. Compare transformed control points with a trusted GIS tool.

For small teaching inputs, converting a selected table to RFC 7946 GeoJSON through an approved conversion tool is a simpler first exercise. Do not claim native GeoPackage support merely because an export can be loaded.

## GDAL — controlled preprocessing

**Goal:** prepare large or unfamiliar source formats outside the UI rendering loop.

1. Use the organization's approved GDAL distribution. Keep its executables, native libraries, PROJ data, and driver versions together. Verify `ogrinfo --version` and the relevant driver's availability.
2. Inspect a source layer and its CRS before converting. Example commands below are **GDAL shell commands**, not application features:

```powershell
ogrinfo -so -al source.gpkg
ogr2ogr -f GeoJSON -t_srs EPSG:4326 -lco RFC7946=YES training-export.geojson source.gpkg selected_table
```

Use a new destination filename. Inspect feature count, bounds, and representative geometries before importing into the demo.

3. If later integrating conversion into the app, execute a configured tool path with `ProcessStartInfo.ArgumentList`; do not concatenate untrusted filenames into a shell command.
4. Run conversion off the dispatcher, capture bounded diagnostic output, allow cancellation, and preserve the original source.
5. Test filenames with spaces, invalid CRS, missing projection grids, unavailable drivers, cancellation, and nonzero exit codes. Log operation results without recording sensitive paths or features.

## Persistent offline cache — separate network isolation from disconnection

**Goal:** allow a deliberately prepared map area to remain available when internal services are unreachable.

1. Define user-selected area, scales, layers, storage limit, expiry, and whether the service permits caching. Air-gapped access to an internal server does not require prefetching by itself.
2. Start with a WMTS tile cache; include service/profile identity, layer, style, format, matrix-set identifier, matrix level, row/column, and authorization scope in the cache key.
3. Store a manifest with extent, CRS, creation time, provenance, and completeness. Never show a partial package as a complete offline map.
4. Separate authenticated users' caches. Credentials are never cache keys or stored payload metadata. Define invalidation and deletion behavior explicitly.
5. Add explicit online/offline mode and a visible missing-data indication. Bound disk use and concurrent downloads; do not launch an unbounded tile crawl.
6. Test disconnected restart, missing tiles, expired entries, changed styles, user changes, interrupted prefetch, and full disk. Check both rendered coverage and manifest counts.

WFS snapshots need their own data-version and edit-conflict policies. WMS viewport-image caching is not interchangeable with WMTS tile caching.

## Search — visible-data search first

**Goal:** find features by their displayed attributes and navigate to them.

1. Add an `IFeatureSearch` interface and a local implementation over loaded vector features. Return a stable feature reference, layer identity, label, and extent.
2. Clearly label the first implementation "Search loaded features"; a bounded WFS viewport is not a complete catalog.
3. Keep filtering and result limits explicit. For server search, discover supported filters and use standard OGC Filter Encoding rather than assuming GeoServer CQL support.
4. Debounce input, cancel previous requests, and reuse the generation-check pattern. Do not block typing on network work.
5. Test duplicate labels, missing attributes, Unicode text, empty results, stale requests, and selecting a result whose layer was removed.

## Editing — local geometry before remote transactions

**Goal:** edit a local copy with undo/redo, validation, and explicit save.

1. Keep selection, editing, and persisted source geometry separate. Begin with adding/moving points; extend to lines/polygons only after undo is reliable.
2. Define commands containing old/new geometry, stable feature identity, and layer identity. Reproject at input/output boundaries.
3. Validate topology before committing edits. Invalid polygon rings must not silently reach the data store.
4. Save a new local GeoJSON file with confirmed destination semantics before attempting server writes. The core demo currently never writes back to an input dataset.
5. Add WFS-T only when the service advertises it and a separate explicit authorization/conflict design exists. Reading WFS does not imply write access.
6. Test undo/redo, canceled edits, invalid geometry, lost selection, save failure, concurrent modification, and CRS round trips.

## Enterprise server integration and deployment

The generic client contract is capabilities plus data requests. Ask the provider's administrator for advertised CRS/format support, layer permissions, bounded WFS access, correct externally advertised operation URLs, and reliable HTTPS. GeoServer can satisfy this contract, but the client does not need its REST API, configuration files, or a PostGIS account.

Production extensions include Windows Integrated Authentication or OAuth, enterprise proxy handling, credential-vault integration, signed packaging, automated update policy, log retention, and accessibility/scaling testing. Implement each against the actual organization's contract and validate it separately. Do not add server provisioning to this client tutorial.
``````

<a id="file-docs-requirements-md-raw"></a>

### `docs/requirements.md`

Source SHA-256: `4bf5a6f6ec23a58021b8b30e9c6739244f1bb6ece64e22b5957b31f59118552f`

``````text
# Requirements coverage

The project follows the supplied email's application goals, with the agreed client-only OGC boundary and advanced topics as extension lessons.

| Requirement | Delivered coverage |
|---|---|
| Architecture and rationale | Lesson 01, project map, pinned API reference |
| Complete solution, csproj, App/MainWindow files | Complete generated source listings and runnable projects |
| MVVM, DI, logging, appsettings | Lesson 03 and configuration reference |
| Layer tree and dynamic management | Lesson 06; visibility, opacity, order, zoom, add/remove |
| WMS | Lesson 07; 1.1.1/1.3.0, map images, conditional GetFeatureInfo |
| WMTS | Lesson 08; 1.0.0, explicit style/matrix-set selection |
| WFS | Lesson 09; 1.1/2.0, bounded GML 3.1/3.2 subset |
| GeoJSON and graphics | Lesson 05; local points, lines, polygons and attributes |
| Selection and highlighting | Independent cloned selection overlay |
| Authentication | Anonymous/Basic over HTTPS; all request stages |
| Coordinate transformations | 4326/3857 plus tested protocol axis-order rules |
| GeoPackage recommendations | Advanced extension design and acceptance cases |
| SQLite and GDAL integration | Advanced extension designs, boundaries, and GDAL command example |
| Offline cache, search, editing | Advanced extension designs; not core implementations |
| Deployment and production guidance | Lesson 11 and enterprise integration extension |
| Troubleshooting and performance | Lesson 10, bounds, debounce and failure tests |
| GeoServer best practices | Reframed as generic OGC client/provider contract; server administration excluded |
| PostGIS | Possible server-side data store; no client database access |
| Package reference list | Project files, API reference, per-project locks |
| Human and local-LLM documentation | Eleven numbered lessons, compact LLM context, combined source-inclusive manual |

The local fixture server is test infrastructure only. It is not a substitute GIS server or an additional deployment dependency. Actual work-service URLs, authentication, data, and Artifactory access have not been supplied to this workspace.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-01-foundations-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/01-foundations.md`

Source SHA-256: `75fd78860e4daff92adb665055f7b735dd9f1b6a0ace4f51c51b0955d7c85965`

``````text
# 01 — Understand the architecture and prepare the workstation

## Purpose and prerequisites

Build a GIS desktop client whose first successful run needs no server. You need basic C#, a supported Windows x64 workstation (Windows 11 recommended), a .NET 10 SDK from your internal software channel, and read access to your internal NuGet v3 feed. An editor is optional; Visual Studio with .NET desktop development is convenient. WSL can hold source and run core tests, but cannot execute WPF.

The original brief named GeoServer and PostGIS. In this client tutorial, those are possible infrastructure behind the OGC endpoint, not client dependencies. The application never assumes a GeoServer workspace URL convention or opens a PostGIS connection.

## Architecture

```text
WPF controls → MainViewModel → local feature services / OGC adapters
                    ↓                         ↓
                 Mapsui map           bounded HTTP transport
                    ↓                         ↓
          Skia rendering + NTS       existing WMS / WMTS / WFS
```

WPF supplies the window, binding, controls, and dispatcher. Mapsui draws maps and handles navigation. NetTopologySuite represents vector geometry. BruTile reads WMTS matrix definitions and forms tile URLs. MVVM separates the view from commands and state. Dependency injection creates the same configured services once per application lifetime.

WMS returns a georeferenced image for an extent; WMTS returns prearranged tiles; WFS returns feature geometry and attributes. These three protocols solve different problems and cannot be substituted merely by changing a URL.

## Step by step

1. Place the source in a Windows-local directory, for example `C:\dev\mapui-wpf-demo`. Avoid building simultaneously from Windows and WSL into the same `obj` directory.
2. Open Windows PowerShell in that directory.
3. Check tooling:

```powershell
dotnet --info
dotnet --list-sdks
dotnet --list-runtimes
```

4. Confirm an SDK in the 10.0 family is available. `global.json` starts at 10.0.100 and allows later stable feature bands. The runtime-only installation cannot compile the solution.
5. Read `OgcDemo.slnx`. It contains Core, Wpf, Tests, and ManualBuilder. Core uses `net10.0`; Wpf uses `net10.0-windows10.0.19041.0` and `win-x64`. The explicit Windows target selects the modern SkiaSharp WPF assets.

## How to use the lessons

The reference-first path is to build the complete solution in lesson 02, then study and exercise one subsystem per lesson. Each lesson includes the complete files it explains. Files later in the series are dependencies of the complete application, so the lessons are not independent partial-project snapshots.

For reconstruction from a single Markdown document, create directories matching the source headings and save **all** source/configuration listings before the first full build. The final appendix includes tests, scripts, and lock files. Do not copy generated `bin` or `obj` directories between operating systems.

## Checkpoint and failure diagnosis

Success means `dotnet --info` identifies Windows x64 and a .NET 10 SDK. If SDK resolution fails, use an approved SDK matching `global.json`; do not remove the framework requirement. If you only have WSL, continue reading and run core tests there, but leave desktop verification explicitly pending.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-02-build-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/02-build.md`

Source SHA-256: `b949b9f7ba97ba2f71368adaf5c91b0198a60a6992909b5160997996aa0fa4f9`

``````text
# 02 — Restore, build, and launch

## Purpose and prerequisites

Turn the source into a running desktop application using only internal package infrastructure. Complete lesson 01 and obtain your organization's Artifactory NuGet v3 endpoint. The checked-in URL is intentionally unusable until configured.

## Step by step (Windows PowerShell)

1. Make the private feed configuration:

```powershell
Copy-Item NuGet.Config NuGet.local.Config
notepad NuGet.local.Config
```

Replace the `example.invalid` URL. Preserve `<clear />` so NuGet cannot inherit a public source. If authentication is required, use the organization's credential provider; see the configuration reference. Do not add credentials to the tracked template.

2. Restore with the checked-in lock files:

```powershell
dotnet restore OgcDemo.slnx --configfile NuGet.local.Config --locked-mode
```

The lock files pin transitive packages as well as direct references. A missing package in Artifactory is a feed preparation problem, not a reason to turn on public fallback. A content-hash mismatch should be investigated rather than bypassed.

3. Build and launch without triggering another restore:

```powershell
dotnet build OgcDemo.slnx --no-restore
dotnet run --project src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-build --no-restore
```

4. Look for the blue application header, two local layers, synthetic geometries, and the feature-details panel. The absence of a public basemap is intentional: the neutral background proves that local content works independently of network services.
5. Run the tests after closing the app:

```powershell
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore
```

## Recreating the skeleton in an empty directory

The equivalent starting commands below show how the solution is assembled. Run them only in a **new empty directory**, then replace the generated files with the complete listings from this guide; do not overwrite the supplied reference project with templates.

```powershell
dotnet new sln -n OgcDemo --format slnx
dotnet new classlib -n OgcDemo.Core -o src/OgcDemo.Core -f net10.0 --no-restore
dotnet new wpf -n OgcDemo.Wpf -o src/OgcDemo.Wpf -f net10.0 --no-restore
dotnet sln OgcDemo.slnx add src/OgcDemo.Core src/OgcDemo.Wpf
dotnet add src/OgcDemo.Wpf reference src/OgcDemo.Core
```

The complete source includes the remaining test/tool projects. The supplied project files, not unversioned `dotnet add package` commands, establish the dependency versions.

## Checkpoint

Build succeeds, the local demo opens, and no OGC endpoint is contacted until you explicitly connect a configured service. NU1701 warnings about SkiaSharp indicate an incorrect Windows target version; preserve the complete WPF project file. WPF cannot be launched with Linux `dotnet`, even when cross-compilation succeeds.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-03-application-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/03-application.md`

Source SHA-256: `653d78e6dabe78467706e8956bc35f05968cbbb6705918dc2f507ba8833cda1d`

``````text
# 03 — Compose the application with MVVM, DI, and logging

## Purpose and prerequisites

Understand who owns configuration, state, and lifecycle. Begin with a successful local run from lesson 02. The complete App, view-model, and window files below show the actual wiring; later lessons explain individual commands and adapters.

## Step by step

1. Read `App.xaml`. There is no `StartupUri`: the dependency-injection container constructs the main window.
2. In `App.xaml.cs`, follow startup: create a host rooted at the executable directory, clear inherited configuration sources, read base JSON plus optional local JSON, register options and the singleton view model/window, then show the window.
3. Read `MainWindow.xaml`. Buttons bind to commands generated by CommunityToolkit.Mvvm. Text fields bind to observable state. The map control itself is assigned by the small code-behind adapter.
4. Read `MainViewModel`. It owns the map and layer collection, and exposes commands such as Home, OpenGeoJson, Connect, and Refresh. Network work uses `await`; map/layer mutations resume on the WPF dispatcher.
5. Observe how closing the window cancels active work and disposes the map and transports. A background continuation must not update a closed application.
6. Open the daily log after running the application:

```powershell
Get-ChildItem "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\OgcDemo\logs"
Get-Content (Get-ChildItem "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\OgcDemo\logs\*.log" | Sort-Object LastWriteTime | Select-Object -Last 1).FullName
```

The log records authored events and exception type names, not service URLs, passwords, server bodies, or selected attributes. Mapsui diagnostics are routed through the same policy. This is a small teaching sink; production logging needs an explicit retention policy.

## Why the view adapter exists

Map taps contain screen/world positions and a renderer-provided hit-test function. Converting that UI event into `SelectAsync` belongs at the view boundary. The view model handles selection state and remote identification. Tree selection similarly assigns the selected layer without embedding GIS operations in XAML code-behind.

Commands generated from `async Task` disable concurrent execution of that command. WFS refresh can also be triggered by navigation, so it has its own cancellation and generation guard; command disabling alone is insufficient.

## Checkpoint

Changing observable status updates the status bar; selecting a feature updates the details panel. Launching from a different working directory still finds configuration beside the executable. Closing the window ends the demo process. If startup fails, inspect the documented prerequisites and JSON syntax; the application deliberately does not reveal arbitrary exception payloads in its normal error dialog.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-04-coordinates-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/04-coordinates.md`

Source SHA-256: `d76afff3cfddf480b90a9bd169933da30b55ed104ab3fc9d3567b16e644c1ee1`

``````text
# 04 — Navigate and transform coordinates

## Purpose and prerequisites

Learn the difference between geographic coordinates, map coordinates, and screen coordinates. Use the running local demo. No remote service is needed.

GeoJSON positions use longitude then latitude in degrees. The map uses EPSG:3857 in metres. A mouse position uses pixels relative to the map control. Never pass one of these coordinate types where another is expected.

## Step by step

1. Inspect `Demo.InitialExtent`: west, south, east, north, in longitude/latitude degrees.
2. Follow `Coordinates.ProjectExtent`: validate the extent, transform both corners, create a Mapsui `MRect` in map metres.
3. Click Home. `Navigator.ZoomToBox` chooses a resolution that fits the transformed extent.
4. Pan and zoom with the mouse, then use the +/− toolbar buttons. Rotation is locked so WMS identification uses the same unrotated bounding box as the visible image.
5. Read `Coordinates.ToMercator`. It copies geometry and changes every coordinate through an NTS coordinate-sequence filter. Setting `SRID = 3857` alone would only relabel the geometry and is incorrect.
6. Run focused checks:

```powershell
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore --filter "FullyQualifiedName~Projection|FullyQualifiedName~AxisOrder|FullyQualifiedName~InvalidCoordinates"
```

## Axis order

For EPSG:4326, WMS 1.3.0 uses latitude/longitude axis order in BBOX; WMS 1.1.1 uses longitude/latitude. The core WFS 1.1/2.0 path also respects the CRS axis order. The actual remote-layer demo requests EPSG:3857, which keeps x/y ordering consistent. These rules do not change GeoJSON's longitude/latitude order.

The axis-order helper is tested even though service rendering is restricted to 3857. Do not solve axis problems by indiscriminately swapping all coordinates. Check the protocol version, CRS identifier, and encoding first.

Web Mercator excludes the poles and distorts distance and area. The application rejects latitude outside its valid range instead of producing infinities. The core has no measurement tool and does not present Web Mercator area as ground-truth area. Local national grids and dateline-crossing extents require explicit extensions.

## Checkpoint

Round-trip projection checks pass. A feature near longitude 35 degrees has an x coordinate of millions of metres, not 35 metres. Home restores the expected synthetic training area. If data appears far away, inspect source CRS before modifying navigation.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-05-local-features-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/05-local-features.md`

Source SHA-256: `c5dca14d73c39e1f351f7b832360eb2d7b75775886446991e9ecf633b3a2751e`

``````text
# 05 — Build graphics overlays and load GeoJSON

## Purpose and prerequisites

Create visible vector data without contacting an OGC service. Complete the coordinate lesson first.

A Mapsui `MemoryLayer` holds `IFeature` instances. `GeometryFeature` wraps NetTopologySuite geometry. A layer style supplies default point, line, and polygon appearance. These are actual vector features; their attributes and geometry remain available after rendering.

## Step by step

1. Read `FeatureLayers.Graphics`. It constructs a point, a line, and a polygon from invented coordinates. It transforms them to 3857 and attaches a human-readable `name` attribute.
2. Click Add graphics. A new memory layer appears at the top of the user-layer list. Use the selected-layer controls to hide or remove it; the bundled source layer remains independent.
3. Open `samples/sites.geojson` in a text editor. Its three features illustrate a point, line, and polygon with a hole. All names and locations are invented training data.
4. Click Open GeoJSON and choose that file. The importer validates limits and geometry, copies attributes, and transforms all coordinates before adding the layer.
5. Click the polygon interior and compare with its hole. A hole is not a separate feature and should remain unfilled.
6. Test the importer:

```powershell
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore --filter "FullyQualifiedName~GeoJson|FullyQualifiedName~Highlight"
```

## Implementation details that matter

The importer accepts a FeatureCollection, not an arbitrary JSON document. The reference uses `NetTopologySuite.IO.GeoJSON4STJ` to avoid hand-parsing nested coordinate arrays. The constructor takes geometry that is already in map coordinates. A shared theme chooses SymbolStyle for points and VectorStyle for lines/polygons. Applying SymbolStyle to every geometry would draw vertex symbols instead of line strokes and polygon fills. Styles are reused rather than allocated for every frame.

The importer rejects a legacy `crs` member because RFC 7946 fixes the coordinate convention. Convert non-4326 source data before loading it. Unsupported or invalid data produces a visible import error without deleting existing layers.

Import and parsing occur asynchronously. There is an 8 MiB and 10,000-feature teaching limit. Large datasets need spatial indexing and viewport loading; do not remove these bounds merely to demonstrate a large file.

## Checkpoint

You can load the sample twice as independent layers, toggle either one, and select a geometry. Removing an imported layer does not change the file on disk. The map remains usable if a later import fails.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-06-layers-selection-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/06-layers-selection.md`

Source SHA-256: `0ed8a6221aaf6662fbc560bfb8d01959679fd30f1488de555194829f1a4dac8f`

``````text
# 06 — Manage layers, select features, and highlight geometry

## Purpose and prerequisites

Connect the layer tree to the rendered map and distinguish selection state from source data. Use only the bundled local layers for this lesson.

## Step by step

1. Expand Map layers. The tree lists user layers **top first**, while Mapsui's collection draws bottom first. Read the index conversion in the move command.
2. Uncheck a layer. Its `Enabled` value changes and the map refreshes. Check it again.
3. Select the layer's tree row (click the row area beside its checkbox). Drag Selected layer opacity. The slider binds to a value between zero and one.
4. Use Up and Down. Confirm that overlapping geometry follows the new drawing order.
5. Use Zoom. The command fits the selected layer extent with a small margin. An empty WFS layer has no extent until data arrives.
6. Click a visible point. The map-control hit test returns a feature from explicitly supplied memory layers. The details panel shows its scalar attributes.
7. Read `FeatureLayers.Select`. It clones the geometry, clears copied feature styles, and puts the clone in a separate selection layer. The original geometry and attributes are unchanged.
8. Click Clear selection, then remove a selected user layer. The selection must not remain as an orphan highlight.

```powershell
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore --filter "FullyQualifiedName~Highlight|FullyQualifiedName~LayerOrdering|FullyQualifiedName~OldRequest"
```

## Design choices

The selection layer is always above user layers and is excluded from hit testing and the layer tree. Otherwise users could select the highlight instead of the original feature. `MainViewModel` owns a single selection for clarity; multiple selection is an extension.

Layer addition/removal is dynamic. Configured OGC profiles are chosen from the service selector, GeoJSON comes from a file dialog, and generated graphics come from a command. Profile authoring remains in JSON rather than a complex connection-editor UI.

A disabled WFS layer stops participating in refreshes. Layer changes invalidate outstanding generations so an old request cannot reintroduce removed data. WMS and WMTS images do not expose local geometry for this picking path; the WMS lesson uses GetFeatureInfo.

## Checkpoint

Visibility, opacity, order, zoom, removal, and clear-selection work without network access. Orange highlighting follows the source feature shape, including polygon holes. The application never saves these temporary selection changes into the input GeoJSON file.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-07-wms-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/07-wms.md`

Source SHA-256: `3d939570caaa95f189a1d5d94a705fa420a3c419436d90e82e53364c48722984`

``````text
# 07 — Connect WMS and identify map images

## Purpose and prerequisites

Display a raster map from an existing internal WMS and request information at a clicked pixel. Obtain its HTTPS endpoint, advertised layer name, supported style, and a layer that offers EPSG:3857. No server administration is part of this lesson.

## Step by step

1. Copy the entire base configuration to the private override:

```powershell
Copy-Item src/OgcDemo.Wpf/appsettings.json src/OgcDemo.Wpf/appsettings.local.json
notepad src/OgcDemo.Wpf/appsettings.local.json
```

Do this only once; do not overwrite an existing local configuration. Replace the WMS placeholder URL and layer name, set `Enabled` to true, and choose `Version` 1.3.0 or 1.1.1 according to the service. Keep `Crs` as EPSG:3857 and `Format` as an advertised image type.

2. Build again so the local configuration is copied to the output directory, then launch:

```powershell
dotnet build src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-restore
dotnet run --project src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-build --no-restore
```

3. Select the profile and click Connect selected service. The adapter downloads capabilities, checks the version/layer/CRS/format, and constructs a Mapsui WmsProvider and ImageLayer.
4. Move the WMS layer below the local overlays if you want it to act as a basemap. Pan and zoom; new GetMap requests include the viewport BBOX and image dimensions.
5. Select the WMS tree row and click a place without a local vector feature. If the layer advertises GetFeatureInfo with text/plain or XML output, its response appears as literal text in the details panel.
6. Run the WMS fixture tests:

```powershell
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore --filter "FullyQualifiedName~Wms|FullyQualifiedName~Authentication"
```

## Understand the requests

GetCapabilities describes the service. GetMap returns image pixels, with LAYERS, STYLES, CRS/SRS, BBOX, WIDTH, HEIGHT, and FORMAT. GetFeatureInfo adds QUERY_LAYERS, INFO_FORMAT, and I/J in WMS 1.3.0 or X/Y in 1.1.1. The identifier request must describe the same viewport as the displayed image.

Queryable and supported CRS can be inherited from a parent capabilities layer. The adapter checks that hierarchy. An ordinary `<Exception>` capabilities section advertises response formats; it is not itself a service error. Only an actual exception-report document triggers the service-error path.

A raster identify response does not contain guaranteed geometry. This demo displays text safely; it does not execute returned HTML or invent a vector highlight for a pixel. A local vector hit takes precedence over raster identification.

## Checkpoint

The WMS image aligns with the graphics, and identify works when advertised. A bad layer or unsupported CRS yields an actionable error. Do not confuse successfully reading capabilities with successfully rendering GetMap; verify both. Real work-service testing is a separate acceptance step from the bundled fixtures.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-08-wmts-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/08-wmts.md`

Source SHA-256: `eee3425ee72b7a875035da2b294626ade9f1765508a72dfdc39db141901d5e75`

``````text
# 08 — Select a WMTS tile matrix set

## Purpose and prerequisites

Add a tile service without assuming its matrix identifiers look like public XYZ zoom levels. Obtain an internal WMTS 1.0.0 capabilities endpoint and an advertised EPSG:3857 matrix set.

## Step by step

1. Edit the existing private configuration. Enable the WMTS profile and fill in its URL, exact layer identifier, style identifier, image format, and matrix-set identifier.
2. Rebuild and launch from Windows PowerShell:

```powershell
dotnet build src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-restore
dotnet run --project src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-build --no-restore
```

3. Choose that profile and connect. The adapter parses capabilities with BruTile and selects a source by **layer + style + format + matrix set**. Human titles are not stable identifiers.
4. Move the tile layer beneath vector overlays. Zoom between several scales and observe tile replacement.
5. Temporarily use an invalid MatrixSet identifier, restart, and reconnect. The demo must reject it rather than silently choosing a different source.
6. Run the focused tests:

```powershell
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore --filter "FullyQualifiedName~Wmts|FullyQualifiedName~UnsupportedServiceCrs"
```

## How tile delivery works

A WMTS matrix defines scale denominator, origin, tile dimensions, matrix width/height, and a matrix identifier. BruTile translates viewport requests into those matrix coordinates and uses the advertised ResourceURL or KVP operation endpoint. This is why the application does not construct a guessed `/z/x/y.png` path.

The source wrapper retains BruTile's URL construction but routes tile bytes through the same bounded, authenticated transport used for capabilities. Authenticating only the capabilities request is insufficient; every protected tile request needs authentication too.

The demo supports 3857 raster matrix sets and rejects other CRSs. It does not reproject cached raster tiles. Persistent caching and disconnected prefetch are extension topics; ordinary in-memory rendering caches are not an offline dataset.

## Checkpoint

Tiles render at multiple scales, align with local vectors, and use the configured style and matrix set. Authentication tests verify both discovery and tile requests. Missing tiles may be outside advertised matrix limits or the data coverage; check capabilities and requested matrix identifiers before changing the origin arithmetic.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-09-wfs-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/09-wfs.md`

Source SHA-256: `3ca0b3c08f0937b0bef58bfac745f2ff290231f79ee4299af6f45d964e0bccf4`

``````text
# 09 — Retrieve WFS features within the current view

## Purpose and prerequisites

Load server-side vector geometry for selection and attribute inspection. Obtain an internal WFS 1.1.0 or 2.0.0 endpoint offering a qualified feature type, EPSG:3857, and GML 3.1/3.2 output. The service must support HTTP GET for the operations used here.

## Step by step

1. Enable and configure the WFS profile in the private settings. Use the exact type name, including its namespace prefix when advertised. Start with MaxFeatures 1000 and the default response-size bound.
2. Rebuild, launch, and connect as in lesson 07.
3. Observe the initialization requests: GetCapabilities verifies version, type, CRS, and output format; DescribeFeatureType retrieves and checks that an XML schema was returned. The demo does not attempt a general schema-driven feature editor.
4. Pan to the data coverage and wait briefly. After a 300 ms debounce, GetFeature requests the current BBOX in 3857 with `count` (2.0) or `maxFeatures` (1.1).
5. Click a returned vector feature. Its geometry is highlighted and its scalar attributes are displayed, just like a local feature.
6. Pan rapidly. Old requests are canceled, and generation checks prevent late completions from replacing the new viewport data.
7. Run focused tests:

```powershell
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore --filter "FullyQualifiedName~Wfs|FullyQualifiedName~Gml|FullyQualifiedName~OldRequest"
```

## Supported GML and limits

The parser accepts GML 3.1/3.2 `pos` and `posList` with two-dimensional Point, LineString, Polygon (holes included), MultiPoint, MultiLineString/MultiCurve containing lines, and MultiPolygon/MultiSurface containing polygons. It deliberately rejects 3D, curved segments, surface patches, and GML 2 encodings. Only scalar feature properties are copied; complex application schemas need a dedicated adapter.

The response must use the requested EPSG:3857 coordinates. Setting a geometry SRID does not repair a server that returned degrees. An explicit unexpected CRS is rejected. The HTTP payload bound protects parsing, and a server returning more than the requested feature limit is rejected. A result at the cap is labeled as potentially incomplete; zoom in rather than treating it as a complete dataset.

WFS network and XML handling are in a small application adapter. Mapsui/NTS still own the resulting vector layer and rendering. This avoids the fixed transport behavior of the pinned built-in WFS provider while retaining a readable teaching implementation.

## Checkpoint

Features follow the current viewport, are selectable, and obey the configured limits. Hiding/removing a WFS layer does not allow an outstanding response to restore it. Server exceptions and unsupported encodings produce a visible error while existing local layers remain usable.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-10-failures-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/10-failures.md`

Source SHA-256: `f86a6ab2f52cdeacc8da1d6d5bc5c1a810a6ab04b1d4629605663b348952427b`

``````text
# 10 — Authenticate, diagnose failures, and tune performance

## Purpose and prerequisites

Connect to protected internal services while preserving responsive behavior and useful diagnostics. You need the service's normal access permissions; this tutorial does not provision accounts or change server security.

## Step by step (Windows PowerShell)

1. Set `Authentication` to `Basic` and select a credential prefix in the private settings. Basic authentication requires HTTPS. Leave it as Anonymous when credentials are unnecessary.
2. Obtain credentials through a prompt instead of placing a password in command history:

```powershell
$ogcCredential = Get-Credential
$env:OGC_DEMO_USERNAME = $ogcCredential.UserName
$env:OGC_DEMO_PASSWORD = $ogcCredential.GetNetworkCredential().Password
dotnet run --project src/OgcDemo.Wpf --no-build --no-restore
Remove-Item Env:OGC_DEMO_USERNAME, Env:OGC_DEMO_PASSWORD
$ogcCredential = $null
```

This uses environment variables for a small demo, not a production credential vault. The variables must exist in the process that launches the application. Do not print them or export them into a tutorial bundle.

3. Connect, pan, identify WMS, and retrieve WFS features. All discovery and follow-up requests use the same transport policy.
4. Test an unavailable service or an incorrect profile. The status bar should show the failure without closing the local demo.
5. Run failure tests:

```powershell
dotnet test tests/OgcDemo.Tests --no-restore --filter "FullyQualifiedName~Failure|FullyQualifiedName~Unauthorized|FullyQualifiedName~Cancellation|FullyQualifiedName~Authentication"
```

## Troubleshooting

| Symptom | First checks |
|---|---|
| Restore fails | Correct internal v3 feed, feed access, required package/version present, lock hash |
| Startup fails | .NET Desktop Runtime/SDK, full published folder, valid JSON, samples present |
| HTTP 401/403 | Correct prefix variables, user permissions, advertised operation endpoint |
| TLS failure | Trusted enterprise CA and correct server name; do not disable validation |
| Different-origin error | Capabilities advertise another scheme/host/port; use an approved same-origin gateway |
| Empty WMS | Layer/style spelling, supported CRS, coverage, transparency, image format |
| Wrong placement | Source CRS, 4326 axis order, protocol version, not just the declared SRID |
| WMTS missing | Exact layer/style/matrix combination and matrix coverage |
| WFS unsupported | Advertised output format, GML encoding, dimension, geometry subset |
| Feature limit reached | Zoom in; the view is incomplete, not a full export |
| Freeze or excess requests | Look for UI-thread blocking, removed debounce, or repeated connection attempts |

## Performance decisions

Keep payload/feature limits; retrieve WFS by viewport. Do not load a whole enterprise dataset just to display one town. Keep shared styles, use WMTS when the server offers suitable tiles, and avoid unnecessary layer refreshes. For larger vectors, introduce spatial indexing and simplification with accuracy tests. Measure request time, response size, feature count, and UI responsiveness separately; this demo does not claim a throughput benchmark.

Each connection has a whole-request timeout and byte limit. Redirects are rejected, certificates are normally validated, XML DTDs are prohibited, and Basic credentials are confined to the configured origin. Safe log messages describe operation and exception type without echoing arbitrary server text.

## Checkpoint

Authentication reaches both capabilities and data requests. Failure tests pass, the log does not contain the synthetic secret payload, and an unavailable service leaves local layers usable. Air-gapped means no internet access; it does not mean internal OGC services are unavailable.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-11-deployment-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/11-deployment.md`

Source SHA-256: `cad75fe3f503f75c9f2c00fe7745289711f51aa49768cc2976f9108689141af1`

``````text
# 11 — Validate and publish a Windows desktop build

## Purpose and prerequisites

Produce a repeatable deliverable and state its validation limits accurately. Use a Windows workstation with the approved .NET 10 SDK and configured Artifactory feed. Keep work configuration separate from the distributable application.

## Step by step (Windows PowerShell)

1. Run the complete build script:

```powershell
.\scripts\Build.ps1 -Publish
```

It restores in locked mode, builds Release, runs tests, checks generated documentation, and publishes a framework-dependent Windows x64 application to `.artifacts/publish`.

2. Check the target workstation has the .NET 10 **Desktop Runtime**, not only the ASP.NET or base runtime. For an optional self-contained build, use the following; Artifactory must contain the relevant runtime packs, and the build may update runtime-specific restore assets:

```powershell
dotnet publish src/OgcDemo.Wpf -c Release -r win-x64 --self-contained true --configfile NuGet.local.Config -p:RestoreLockedMode=true -o .artifacts/publish-self-contained
```

3. Copy the entire publish folder to a user-writable test directory. Do not copy only the EXE; native SkiaSharp assets, assemblies, JSON, and samples are required. Trimming and single-file deployment are deliberately not enabled.
4. Place a reviewed `appsettings.local.json` beside the EXE on the destination only when needed. The project excludes private settings from publish output.
5. Run the opt-in Windows integration smoke check with a fresh report name:

```powershell
.\scripts\Test-Desktop.ps1 -Report .artifacts/desktop-smoke-new.json
```

The application creates its real window, exercises its in-process commands and rendered hit testing, checks the unavailable-service path, exports an image, writes a JSON report, and exits. This is not a physical mouse/keyboard walkthrough.
6. Perform the human acceptance pass: pan/wheel zoom, open a local GeoJSON file through the dialog, click shapes/holes, adjust tree controls, resize the window, connect your work services, and verify their real data and authentication. Record server/protocol versions and results without including credentials.

## Production deployment recommendations

Distribute through the organization's approved packaging/signing process. Treat the shipped version and dependency lock as a unit. Keep private configuration and credentials out of packages and source bundles. Choose a writable log directory and define retention explicitly; the demo's daily files do not delete old logs. Test display scaling, graphics drivers, enterprise proxy/TLS behavior, actual layer permissions, and payload sizes on representative workstations.

Windows Integrated Authentication, OAuth/SSO, credential-vault storage, signed installers, updates, and production telemetry are extension work. Do not claim the demo is a finished enterprise product.

## Checkpoint

The published application starts with its local layers, the smoke report records success, and the documentation check reports no stale outputs. Report core/fixture tests, Windows integration checks, manual desktop checks, and actual work-service checks as separate evidence. A successful HTTP response alone is not proof that a map is geographically correct.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-12-traffic-viewer-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/12-traffic-viewer.md`

Source SHA-256: `0f152e7dd7d04ab4aba58a7917ed0085a89a290d40a0744abe7087413e51cc50`

``````text
# 12. AIS and ADS-B traffic viewer

The **Traffic Lab** is a third, independent WPF application in this solution. It
receives simulated decoded ship and aircraft reports and measures the cost of
tracking and drawing them in Mapsui. It does not decode radio messages or connect
to real receivers. Every identity and route is synthetic; the authored sea routes
are demonstration waypoints, not navigation data.

The engineering target is 10,000 simultaneous objects. **10,000 is not an enforced
maximum.** Higher nonnegative counts are accepted with a visible warning. The
mailbox and track capacity grow to the configured combined population, so increasing
counts also increases the memory budget. Counts must fit a signed 32-bit total;
this is a representation limit, not a promise that such a population is practical.

## Build and run on Windows

Use the existing .NET 10 SDK/internal NuGet feed instructions from lesson 02.
The Windows Desktop runtime is required; Linux can compile the Windows target but
cannot execute WPF. Keep the repository's pinned package versions and lock files.

```powershell
dotnet restore OgcDemo.slnx --configfile NuGet.local.Config --locked-mode
dotnet build OgcDemo.slnx -c Release --no-restore
dotnet run --project src/TrafficDemo.Wpf -c Release --no-build --no-restore
```

To open an interactive session with 5,000 ships and 5,000 aircraft already running:

```powershell
dotnet run --project src/TrafficDemo.Wpf -c Release --no-build --no-restore -- --ships 5000 --aircraft 5000 --start
```

`--start` begins simulation after the basemap connection attempt and leaves the window
open for interaction. Without it, press Start in the viewer.

To publish all supported full applications and run core checks:

```powershell
./scripts/Build.ps1 -Publish
./scripts/Start-TrafficDemo.ps1
```

The traffic executable is published separately to
`.artifacts/traffic-publish/TrafficDemo.Wpf.exe`. The existing OGC executable keeps
its original output location. Running from source connects to the basemap directly;
the launch helper additionally checks/starts the existing GeoServer deployment.

## GeoServer integration

The default service is `http://127.0.0.1:8602/geoserver`, from the sibling
`small-gis-demos/osm-basemap-demo` project. The server inspected for this work serves
the Eastern Europe bundle, including Greece and the Adriatic coast, with worldwide
Natural Earth overview context. It advertises English Dark Matter, OSM Bright,
Positron, Toner, MapTiler Basic and world layers. The default is
`omt:dark-matter-en`, WMTS 1.0.0, `EPSG:3857`, PNG. BruTile normalizes GeoServer's advertised empty style identifier to `default`; the viewer selects that normalized
style, which this server accepts.
Auxiliary WMTS ResourceURL entries such as TileJSON and FeatureInfo are removed from
the client parser input, leaving only tile templates for the selected image format.
This avoids BruTile 6 treating metadata responses as map images. WMS 1.3.0 is an explicit alternative. `None` selects overlay-only measurement.

The app shares `OgcDemo.Core` transport/capabilities code. Only this viewer opts
into HTTP for loopback endpoints; remote services require HTTPS. Credentials in URLs
and cross-origin capabilities endpoints remain disallowed. No public basemap,
font server, API key or live traffic service is required.

```powershell
# A healthy service is reused, without touching the container engine.
./scripts/Ensure-TrafficBasemap.ps1 -CheckOnly
./scripts/Start-TrafficDemo.ps1

# If the existing deployment is stopped inside WSL, name its distribution.
./scripts/Start-TrafficDemo.ps1 -WslDistribution RHEL10
```

On Linux/WSL, run `bash scripts/ensure-traffic-basemap.sh`. Its default existing
container is `east-europe-bundle_geoserver_1`, using Podman. Override
`TRAFFIC_BASEMAP_CONTAINER` and `TRAFFIC_CONTAINER_ENGINE` for an already prepared
deployment. Windows Podman defaults to `east-europe-bundle`; the PowerShell helper
also accepts an explicit `-ContainerName`. Helpers verify the container's configured
port binding before starting it, including when it is stopped. They do not build or
import a bundle, delete/recreate volumes, pull images, change firewall rules, or start
a Podman machine. If no prepared deployment exists, use that project's documented
bundle launcher first. Starting the app never implicitly provisions infrastructure.

A failed basemap connection leaves simulation usable against the plain background.
The status displays the failure and the **Connect / retry** button retries. A
successful capabilities connection alone is not proof of tile delivery; the benchmark
also records basemap request failures, and the captured map must be inspected.

## Operator workflow

1. Set ship and aircraft counts independently, or stage the 1,000 / 5,000 / 10,000
   mixed presets. Defaults are 500 of each. Preset buttons do not discard a running
   simulation until **Apply configuration and restart** is clicked.
2. Set the random seed, report intervals and simulation speed. Defaults are seed
   42, five seconds for ships, two seconds for aircraft and speed 1. Reports are
   staggered, not delivered as a single population-wide burst.
3. Choose the Adriatic/Greece regional scenario or the dense Ionian Sea scenario.
   The dense home view contains the complete population; there is no clustering.
4. Press **Start**. **Pause** freezes simulation time, route movement and ageing;
   **Resume** continues. **Reset** clears history/selection and leaves the new
   population paused. Applying configuration clears old state and starts afresh.
5. Click a symbol or table row. Details show identity, position, speed, course,
   aircraft altitude, report time/sequence and freshness. **Zoom to selected**
   centers a closer view. **Follow** recenters once per published snapshot. Panning,
   touch movement or mouse-wheel navigation suspends following; check Follow to resume.
6. Use the Filters tab to search identities/names or narrow traffic. The track table
   shows display-eligible tracks across the scenario, not just the current viewport.
7. Use Performance for counters and current-report export. A current-report export
   is not a completed benchmark or a soak test.

Symbols rotate in five-degree display increments using shared styles. Actual report
course remains unquantized in the details. Cyan ship silhouettes and amber aircraft
silhouettes distinguish types. Stale symbols/traces fade. Only the selected object
gets a label by default, avoiding thousands of overlapping text labels.

## Two kinds of filtering

Both panels support ship/aircraft admission, case-insensitive identity/name substring,
speed range in knots, aircraft altitude range in feet, and geographic bounds in
`west,south,east,north` longitude/latitude order. Blank values are unrestricted.
Altitude constraints apply only to aircraft. A west value greater than east denotes
a dateline-crossing geographic filter. Invalid or reversed ranges are rejected.

**Input admission** excludes reports before track updates and purges existing tracks
that no longer qualify. Clearing that filter reconstructs tracks only as new reports
arrive; their discarded history is not restored. The simulator still generates the
full configured population, making generated versus accepted load visible.

**Display filtering** hides symbols, traces and table rows while retaining ingestion,
tracking and bounded history. Clearing it restores the retained data. A selected
track hidden by a filter remains identified in the details panel, while its highlight
and following are suspended. Expired/input-purged selected tracks are similarly marked.
Input filtering and display filtering are separate benchmark operations: changing
one must never be reported as changing the other.

## Configuration and units

`src/TrafficDemo.Wpf/appsettings.json` contains a `Traffic` section. Copy it to
`appsettings.local.json` alongside it for private overrides; the latter is ignored by
Git and excluded from publish output. JSON configuration overlays individual keys.
Editing UI controls affects the current process, not the files on disk.

| Section | Important defaults |
| --- | --- |
| Simulation | Ships 500; Aircraft 500; Seed 42; ShipIntervalSeconds 5; AircraftIntervalSeconds 2; SpeedMultiplier 1; Dense false |
| History | MaximumPoints 150; RetentionSeconds 300; SampleSeconds 2; StaleSeconds 30; ExpireSeconds 120 |
| InputFilter / DisplayFilter | Ships and Aircraft true; Search empty; numeric limits and Bounds unset |
| Basemap | Url loopback port 8602; Layer omt:dark-matter-en; Protocol WMTS; Enabled true |

History intervals, feed timestamps, route progress and ageing use simulation time.
The one-second snapshot schedule, benchmark duration and latency measurements use
wall/monotonic time. At simulation speed 10, five simulated seconds pass in roughly
half a real second. Accelerated scenarios therefore increase report load as well as
movement. A report interval of at least 0.1 simulated seconds is required. History
requires at least two points, positive finite intervals and expiry greater than the
stale interval. Configure stale/expiry above your expected report cadence when using
unusually slow sources. MaximumPoints has a defensive 100,000-per-track bound.

The default map update cadence is once per second, with no millisecond animation or
continuous extrapolation. Aircraft move farther between reports than ships; zoom in
or use simulation speed to make ship movement apparent without falsifying their speed.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-13-traffic-data-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/13-traffic-data.md`

Source SHA-256: `88e9ab1cc84b04e1e3385fc8af9311850a2c02768f1d4bb7c387611e7492f465`

``````text
# 13. Bounded traffic pipeline and map rendering

The design separates decoded observations, current track state, bounded history,
renderable map data and WPF controls. No simulation report creates a WPF element.

```text
SimulatedFeed (background producer)
  → validation + latest-report mailbox (one pending report per identity)
  → TrackStore (one background consumer; admission and order checks)
  → bounded trace rings + current state
  → immutable TrafficSnapshot, once per wall-clock second
  → display filter + zoom simplification + spatial indexes (background)
  → one replaceable pending RenderSnapshot
  → one queued Dispatcher publication
  → indexed Mapsui symbol/trace layers → standard Skia renderer
                                      → completed-render measurements
```

## Contracts and ownership

`ITrackFeed.ReadAsync(CancellationToken)` supplies decoded `TrackReport` values. A
report contains type, identity, name, UTC/simulation timestamp, sequence,
longitude/latitude, knots, course degrees and optional altitude feet. Its key combines
type and identity, so a ship and aircraft with the same textual ID remain distinct.
Real wire-protocol adapters are a future extension behind this interface; the supplied
viewer constructs `SimulatedFeed` and is not a raw AIS/ADS-B decoder.

`ReceivedReport` adds a monotonic ingestion timestamp. The report timestamp determines
ordering/history/age; the monotonic timestamp measures elapsed processing time without
wall-clock adjustment errors. `SimulationClock` advances from monotonic elapsed time
and freezes on pause. Equal seed, configuration, epoch and evaluation time produce
identical simulated positions. The live scheduler naturally has operating-system
jitter; determinism refers to route/attribute evaluation, not exact thread scheduling.

The feed writes only the mailbox. The consumer alone owns `TrackStore` and its trace
rings. Filter configurations are immutable values published atomically. Snapshots
contain detached history arrays; code treats all published data and geometry as
read-only. Geometry/index construction happens on the consumer worker. WPF receives
only a completed snapshot and performs one batched replacement and graphics refresh.
Mapsui owns its own render caches; the app does not dispose native resources while
Mapsui might be drawing them. Shutdown cancels and joins workers before disposing map
resources. Resets replace the engine and use generation checks to discard old work.

## Overload and ordering

The mailbox has capacity equal to the configured population, including populations
above 10,000. A newer report replaces a pending report for the same key. Replacement
increments `Coalesced`; an already full mailbox rejects a new identity and increments
`CapacityRejected`. Invalid reports and non-increasing sequences/backward timestamps
are rejected. The tracker repeats ordering validation against the last accepted state,
rejects future/expired observations, and enforces its configured track capacity.

The simulator never emits an unbounded catch-up burst after a scheduling stall. It
emits the current observation and schedules the next due report. This means the
configured interval is a target cadence, not a promise that every report is generated
under saturation. Generated/accepted counters and process CPU must be considered
alongside latency. Coalescing deliberately loses intermediate observations; it is
appropriate for a current-position viewer, not a lossless recorder.

The UI has one pending snapshot slot and at most one queued publication callback.
A newer snapshot replaces an older unpublished one and increments `ReplacedSnapshots`.
The dispatcher probe also awaits its previous callback, preventing a monitoring
backlog from becoming another load source. A slow renderer can increase latency and
coalescing, but cannot create an unbounded queue of UI updates.

## Trace storage and gaps

Each track has a chronological ring capped by both age and number of points. Default
retention is the last five simulated minutes, at most 150 samples, with a minimum
two-second sample spacing. At the 10,000-object target, the strict upper bound is
**1.5 million retained history points**. Five-second ship reporting normally uses
fewer points than the bound. Memory also includes track metadata, detached snapshot
arrays, symbols, line geometry, spatial indexes, WPF rows and Mapsui/Skia caches; do
not equate the point count with total process memory.

Sequence gaps, sufficiently long report gaps and dateline discontinuities mark a
new trace segment. Unsampled reports still update current position; a pending gap
marker survives until the next retained sample. Rendering never joins these segments
across missing observations. Traces end at the latest retained sample, which can lag
the current symbol by the configured sampling interval.

No report for 30 simulated seconds marks a track stale; 120 seconds expires it and
releases its history. History ages out even when no new reports arrive. Pause freezes
these rules rather than making every paused object stale. Clearing input filters does
not recover discarded samples.

## Rendering choices and costs

Two `IndexedTrafficLayer` instances separate symbols and traces. Their immutable
`FeatureIndex` uses an NTS STRtree, built on the worker and queried with the current
viewport. Symbol queries include a screen-size margin. Trace indexes cover the full
polyline envelope, so a line crossing the viewport remains eligible even when its
current symbol and both endpoints are outside it. Envelope intersection is a broad
phase; Mapsui performs final drawing/clipping.

This replaces the per-query collection scan in the pinned
[Mapsui 5.1.0 MemoryLayer implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Mapsui/Mapsui/5.1.0/Mapsui/Layers/MemoryLayer.cs).
The one-object selection highlight still uses `MemoryLayer`; its collection has at
most one feature, so indexing it would add unnecessary work.

The standard Mapsui renderer draws the actual points and NTS multiline geometries.
This tests Mapsui's map/navigation/rendering path rather than a separate overlay canvas.
No per-track WPF control is placed on the map. The data grid enables row/column
virtualization and recycling; it does not instantiate 10,000 visible rows.

Only two SVG image resources are registered. Symbol styles are shared across type,
staleness and 72 heading buckets; trace styles are also shared. This prevents a
per-report image/style registration leak. Per-track immutable feature caching reuses
unchanged symbols and traces. Cache entries are removed when the track leaves the
eligible set; Mapsui's rendering caches have their own bounded eviction policy.

Douglas–Peucker simplification is applied separately to each displayed trace segment.
The tolerance is 0.75 times a power-of-two bucket of map resolution, in projected
units. The original retained history is never simplified. Resolution bucketing avoids
rebuilding all lines for tiny zoom changes; the next snapshot uses the new zoom bucket.
Selected labels are independent of the traffic style cache. Map rotation is handled
by Mapsui's directional style support.

Per report, current-state/history work is approximately constant. At each snapshot,
filtering and index construction inspect the configured track set; line simplification
cost depends on retained points. Viewport rendering cost depends on the eligible
geometry in view. Spatial indexing helps a zoomed-in view, but cannot eliminate the
cost of drawing every object in a dense 10,000-object view. All eligible symbols remain
present, even when their pixels overlap. The performance harness must expose that cost.

`DataHasChanged` and graphics refresh update the overlays. Routine traffic ticks do
not call `Map.Refresh`, which would request basemap data again. Pan/zoom and explicit
basemap connection changes legitimately trigger tile/image requests. GeoServer remains
a basemap server; moving traffic and history are not uploaded to it.

The retained-point count is a **storage** measurement. It is not the number of vertices
submitted to the renderer: zoom-dependent simplification can reduce a straight trace
to its endpoints. Traces-disabled runs still maintain bounded tracking history, which
isolates much of the drawing/geometry cost from source and history ingestion cost.
``````

<a id="file-docs-templates-14-traffic-performance-md"></a>

### `docs/templates/14-traffic-performance.md`

Source SHA-256: `c53c3b60977a453a8503518e42b50d0562227933eaa0cadfaad8e1736abf1230`

``````text
# 14. Measuring traffic performance

Run Release builds on Windows. A successful build or core test cannot establish WPF
rendering performance. Measurements belong to the recorded hardware, viewport/DPI,
population, timing, history and basemap/cache configuration.

## Repeatable commands

```powershell
./scripts/Test-TrafficDesktop.ps1 -Report .artifacts/traffic/smoke-new.json
./scripts/Benchmark-Traffic.ps1 -Seconds 60
./scripts/Benchmark-Traffic.ps1 -Seconds 60 -FullMatrix -Soak
```

Each runner refuses an existing report/output location. The default matrix is 18
sequential cases: 1,000, 5,000 and 10,000 objects; all ships, all aircraft and mixed;
with GeoServer and overlay-only. It uses a dense viewport and traces. `-FullMatrix`
adds dispersed views and trace-disabled comparisons for all populations/types/basemap
modes. The scripted interaction cycle selects/follows a track, returns home, applies
and clears a display filter, and pans. Fixed-view runs are available directly through
the executable. `-Soak` adds a 30-minute, 10,000-object mixed dense run with traces and
GeoServer. Do not run multiple benchmark apps simultaneously for baseline evidence.
Keep the benchmark window visible, use a consistent window size/DPI, and avoid other
heavy workloads during a comparison. Minimizing or manually navigating the window
changes the rendering workload and should be recorded as a separate experiment.

```powershell
.artifacts/traffic-publish/TrafficDemo.Wpf.exe --report C:\Temp\traffic-10k.json --seconds 1800 --ships 5000 --aircraft 5000 --dense
.artifacts/traffic-publish/TrafficDemo.Wpf.exe --report C:\Temp\traffic-overlay.json --seconds 60 --ships 5000 --aircraft 5000 --dense --no-basemap
```

Additional arguments are `--no-traces`, `--exercise`, and `--smoke`. Native runs export
JSON, a CSV process time series and a final PNG. The interactive Performance tab can
export current JSON/CSV without completing a timed test. JSON records the configuration,
viewport, DPI, runtime, processor description, Mapsui version and cache conditions.
The server cache is deliberately not flushed; each new app starts with a new client
cache. This is a repeatable workload, not a claim of a cold server-cache comparison.

## What the measurements mean

| Measurement | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Generated | Reports actually offered by the simulator; cadence may fall under saturation |
| Accepted / rejected | Tracker admissions and validation/filter/order rejections |
| Coalesced | Pending reports superseded by a newer report for the same identity |
| CapacityRejected | New identities rejected by a full mailbox |
| Tracked / filtered / eligible | Current state, excluded display state, and eligible tracks |
| Drawn symbols / traces | Spatial-query candidates submitted to Mapsui for the viewport; overlapping symbols still count individually |
| Ingestion-to-render ms | Monotonic receive timestamp to first completed on-screen Mapsui render for a new visible report |
| Report age seconds | Simulation-time age of reports submitted in a completed frame |
| Preparation / publication ms | Worker geometry/index construction and batched UI publication respectively |
| Render ms | Time inside the actual Mapsui renderer's `Render` call |
| Dispatcher ms | Delay until a background-priority UI callback executes |
| Memory / allocation / GC | Private, working-set and managed bytes; cumulative allocated bytes and GC counts |
| CPU | Process CPU time; dashboard normalizes usage over logical processors |

Preparation timing starts after the tracking-state/history snapshot copy; ingestion-to-render
latency still includes that earlier work.

`MeasuredRenderer` forwards rendering, drawable creation, picking and bitmap export
to the pinned standard renderer. Timing is recorded **after** drawing returns; merely
queuing a refresh is not a completed-frame measurement. It does not measure physical
monitor scan-out. Bitmap export is not counted as an on-screen frame.

Ingestion latency is recorded once per newly rendered track sequence. Reports replaced
before rendering are not latency samples: coalescing and snapshot-replacement counters
must therefore accompany the percentile. Offscreen tracks are not drawn and do not
produce rendering-latency samples until in view. A newly visible report can already be
several seconds old. Report-age measurements include that staleness independently.

Metric buffers retain at most 20,000 recent samples per metric for exact dashboard
percentiles. Separate fixed histograms accumulate the whole run without retaining
individual samples. Benchmark acceptance uses `LifetimeP95UpperBound` and
`LifetimeP99UpperBound`: 1 ms buckets for timings, 0.01 s buckets for report age.
The overflow bucket is above 60 seconds for timings (600 seconds for report age);
a percentile falling there is null and fails the corresponding target. These are
conservative bucket upper bounds. Long-run stability must also be assessed using
the CSV, counters and scenario coverage, rather than a good final-window percentile. Process samples are bounded to
approximately 720 per timed run. Dashboard CPU and allocations include the application,
not just the renderer, and the renderer wrapper itself adds measurement overhead.

## Acceptance targets

For 10,000 objects with normal report intervals and bounded traces:

- p95 ingestion-to-render at most 3 seconds; p99 at most 5 seconds.
- p95 dispatcher response at most 250 ms during the interaction workload.
- No queue, track or retained-history count above its configured bound.
- The full configured population must be tracked; dense-view symbols must be checked
  against that population. Dispersed/zoomed views legitimately draw fewer symbols.
- A 30-minute soak with stable post-warm-up memory. The report calculates a private-byte
  linear trend after minute 10; at most 1 MiB/min is the explicit diagnostic threshold.
  Inspect the time series as well, because a threshold alone cannot prove absence of leaks.

`success` means the run completed its functional checks with a population and native
frames. `acceptance` reports latency, dispatcher, bounds and soak separately. A short
run has `soakEvaluated: false`, not a passing soak. Basemap failures are reported
separately and fail baseline acceptance; a working overlay cannot establish GeoServer success. A failure is not
fixed by silently disabling traces, clustering objects, reducing counts or changing
report intervals. Such changes are distinct, clearly labelled comparison cases.

## Test coverage and troubleshooting

Core tests cover deterministic simulation, speed/direction changes, pause/cancellation,
report validation and ordering, mailbox replacement/capacity, time/count history limits,
sequence/time/dateline gaps, stale/expiry transitions, input versus display semantics,
spatial queries for crossing traces, immutable render snapshots, trace simplification,
metric bounds, engine shutdown and intact 1k/5k/10k/12k populations.

The Windows smoke run verifies native rendering, symbol hit testing, trace publication,
selection, following suspension, pause, display filtering without state loss, input
purging, reset/start, and recoverable GeoServer failure. It is in-process integration,
not physical mouse/keyboard automation. Inspect its PNG for visual correctness and
record separately whether a human or desktop automation exercised physical input.

- **No window/report:** verify `dotnet --list-runtimes` contains both .NET 10 and
  Microsoft.WindowsDesktop.App. An empty leftover SDK directory is not an installation.
- **Plain background:** check both WMS capabilities and WMTS tile delivery from the
  Windows host. A WSL-only curl result does not establish Windows access.
- **No selected track after filtering:** the details explain whether it is hidden,
  purged or expired; clear the relevant filter and wait for new reports where necessary.
- **Traces appear short:** ships move slowly, retained history starts empty, and the
  trace ends at the latest sampled observation. Zoom in, wait, or deliberately change
  simulation speed. Higher speed also increases input load.
- **High memory:** inspect population ×MaximumPoints and GeoServer separately; private
  bytes include native drawing/cache allocations as well as managed objects.
- **High latency or coalescing:** compare preparation, publication and rendering costs;
  compare overlay-only versus GeoServer runs and inspect GC/CPU. Keep all configuration
  differences in the report rather than attributing every delay to Mapsui.

See [validation](../validation.md) for the actual checked-out implementation's results.

Timing/GC counters belong to the application session. Interactive Reset/Apply clears
simulation state and its admission counters, but retains profiler history. Start a
fresh process for configuration comparisons; the benchmark runner does this for every
case. Smoke tests intentionally pause/reset/filter and are functional checks, so their
latency acceptance fields are not representative steady-load performance results.
``````
